(84; original 492): This canon has been recently discovered also in a Coptic translation, and published by Pitra, in the Spiclegium Solesmense, i. 526 sq.

(85; original 493): Kai; th'/ timh'/ prohgei'sqai autovn.

(86; original 494): Cyprian, Epist. 45, says of his province of Carthage: "Latius fusa est nostra provincia; habet enim Numidiam et Mauretaniam sibi cohaerentes."

(87; original 495): Patriavrch"; patriarcha; sometimes also, after the political terminology, e[xarco". The name patriarch, originally applied to the progenitors of Israel (Heb. vii. 4, to Abraham; Acts vii. 8 sq., to the twelve sons of Jacob; ii. 29, to David, as founder of the Davidic Messianic house), was at first in the Eastern church an honorary title for bishops in general (so in Gregory Nazianzen, and Gregory of Nyssa), but after the council of Constantinople (381), and still more after that of Chalcedon (451), it came to be used in an official sense and restricted to the five most eminent metropolitans. In the West, several metropolitans, especially the bishop of Aquileia, bore this title honoris causa. The bishop of Rome declined that particular term, as placing him on a level with other patriarchs, and preferred the name papa. "Patriarch" bespeaks an oligarchical church government; "pope," a monarchical.

(88; original 496): According to the political division of the empire after Constantine. Comp. § 54

(89; original 497): Comp. Wiltsch, i. p. 206 sqq. The statement of Ziegler, which Wiltsch quotes and seems to approve, that the fifth ecumenical council, of 553, added to the patriarchal circuit of Jerusalem the metropolitans of Berytus in Phenicia, and Ruba in Syria, appears to be an error. Ruba nowhere appears in the acts of the council, and Berytus belonged to Phoenicia prima, consequently to the patriarchate of Antioch. Le Quien knows nothing of such an enlargement of the patriarchate of Hierosolyma.

(90; original 498): Wiltsch, i. 189 sqq.

(91; original 499): Ibid. i. 177 sqq.

(92; original 500): Ibid. p. 143 sqq.

(93; original 501): Comp. § 57, below.

(94; original 502): Comp. Wiltsch, i. p. 232 sq., and ii. 469.

(95; original 503): Cod. can. eccl. Afr. can. 39, cited by Neander, iii. p. 335 (Germ. ed.).

(96; original 504): Accordingly Pope Nicolas, in 866, in a letter to the Bulgarian prince Bogoris, would acknowledge only the bishops of Rome, Alexandria, and Antioch as patriarchs in the proper sense, because they presided over apostolic churches; whereas Constantinople was not of apostolic founding, and was not even mentioned by the most venerable of all councils, the Nicene; Jerusalem was named indeed by these councils, but only under the name of Aelia.

(97; original 505): In the oldest Latin Cod. canonum (in Mansi, vi. 1186) this canon is preceded by the important words: Ecclesia Romana semper habuit primatum. These are, however, manifestly spurious, being originally no part of the canon itself, but a superscription, which gave an expression to the Roman inference from the Nicene canon. Comp. Gieseler, i. 2, § 93, note 1; and Hefele, Hist. of Councils, i. 384 sqq.

(98; original 506): So Greenwood also views the matter, Cathedra Petri, 1859, vol. i. p. 181: "It was manifestly not the object of this canon to confer any new jurisdiction upon the church of Alexandria, but simply to confirm its customary prerogative. By way of illustration, it places that prerogative, whatever it was, upon the same level with that of the two other eparchal churches of Rome and Antioch. Moreover, the words of the canon disclose no other ground of claim but custom; and the customs of each eparchia are restricted to the territorial limits of the diocese or eparchia itself. And though, within those limits, the several customary rights and prerogatives may have differed, yet beyond them no jurisdiction of any kind could, by virtue of this canon, have any existence at all."

(99; original 507): Conc. Constant. i. can 3: To;n mevntoi Kwvstantinoupovlew" ejpivskopon e[cein ta; presbei'a th'" timh'", meta; to;n th's JRwvmh" ejpivskopon, dia; to; eiaujth;n nevan JRwvmhn . This canon is quoted also by Socrates, v. 8, and Sozomen, vii. 9, and confirmed by the council of Chalcedon (see below); so that it must be from pure dogmatical bias, that Baronius (Annal. ad ann. 381, n. 35, 36) questions its genuineness

(100; original 508): The latter is not, indeed, expressly said in the above canon, which seems to speak only of an honorary precedence. But the canon was so understood by the bishops of Constantinople, and by the historians Socrates (v. 8) and Theodoret (Epist. 86, ad Flavianum), and so interpreted by the Chalcedonian council (can. 28). The relation of the bishop of Constantinople to the metropolitan of Heraclea, however, remained for a long time uncertain, and at the council ad Quercum, 403, in the affair of Chrysostom, Paul of Heraclea took the presidency, though the patriarch Theophilus of Alexandria was present. Comp. Le Quien, tom. i. p. 18; and Wiltsch, i. p. 139.

(101; original 509): H. E. lib. v. cap. 28.

(102; original 510): According to Sozomen it was thirteen, according to Theophilus of Alexandria at the council ad Quercum seventeen bishops, whom he instituted; and this act was charged against him as an unheard-of crime. See Wiltsch, i. 141.

(103; original 511): Socrates, H. E. l. vii. 28, where such a law is incidentally mentioned. The inhabitants of Cyzicus in the Hellespont, however, transgressed the law, on the presumption that it was merely a personal privilege of Atticus.

(104; original 512): Among the barbarian tribes, over whom the bishops of Constantinople exercised an ecclesiastical jurisdiction, were the Huns on the Bosphorus, whose king, Gorda, received baptism in the time of Justinian; the Herulians, who received the Christian faith in 527; the Abasgians and Alanians on the Euxine sea, who about the same time received priests from Constantinople. Comp. Wiltsch, i. 144 and 145.

(105; original 513): This correction of the Roman legates is so little to the taste of the Roman Catholic historians, especially the ultramontane, that the Ballerini, in their edition of the works of Leo the Great, tom. iii. p. xxxvii. sqq., and even Hefele, Conciliengesch. i. p. 385, and ii. p. 522, have without proof declared the relevant passage in the Greek Acts of the council of Chalcedon a later interpolation. Hefele, who can but concede the departure of the Latin version from the original text of the sixth canon of Nice, thinks, however, that the Greek text was not read in Chalcedon, because even this bore against the elevation of Constantinople, and therefore in favor of the Roman legates. But the Roman legates, as also Leo in his protest against the 28th decree of Chalcedon, laid chief stress upon the Roman addition, Ecclesia Romana semper habuit primatum, and considered the equalization of any other patriarch with the bishop of Rome incompatible with it. Since the legates, as is conceded, appealed to the Nicene canon, the Greeks had first to meet this appeal, before they passed to the canons of the council of Constantinople. Only the two together formed a sufficient answer to the Roman protest.

(106; original 514): Mansi, vii. p. 446-454; Harduin, ii, 639-643; Hefele, ii. 524, 525.

(107; original 515): Leo, Epist. 104, 105, and 106 (al, Ep. 78-80). Comp. Hefele, l.c. ii. 530 sqq.

(108; original 516): Rev. iii. 11.

(109; original 517): Harduin, tom. vii. 23; Schröckh, xvii. 43; and Hefele, ii. 544.

(110; original 518): Comp. Eusebius, himself the metropolitan of Caesarea, H. E. v. 23. He gives the succession of the bishops of Jerusalem, as well as of Rome, Alexandria, and Antioch, while he omits those of Caesarea.

(111; original 519): jAkolouqiva th'" timh'"; which is variously interpreted. Comp. Hefele, i. 389 sq.

(112; original 520): Ta; presbei'a th'" timh'"... dia; to; ei|nai aujth;n [i.e. Constantinople] nevan JRwvmhn. Comp. § 56.

(113; original 521): The title oijkomeniko;" patriavrch", universalis episcopus, had before been used in flattery by oriental patriarchs, and the later Roman bishops bore it, in spite of the protest of Gregory I., without scruple. The statement of popes Gregory I. and Leo IX., that the council of Chalcedon conferred on the Roman bishop Leo the title of universal episcopus, and that he rejected it, is erroneous. No trace of it can be found either in the Acts of the councils or in the epistles of Leo. In the Acts, Leo is styled oJ aJgiwvtato" kai; makariwvtato" ajrciepivskopo" th'" megavlh" kai; presbutevras JRwvmh"; which, however, in the Latin Acts sent by Leo to the Gallican bishops, was thus enlarged: "Sanctus et beatissimus Papa, caput universalis ecclesiae, Leo." The papal legates at Chalcedon subscribed themselves: Vicarii apostolici universalis ecclesiae papae, which the Greeks translated: th'" oijkoumenikh'" ejkklhsiva" ejpiskovpou. Hence probably arose the error of Gregory I. The popes wished to be papae universalis ecclesiae, not episcopi or patriarchae universales; no doubt because the latter designation put them on a level with the Eastern patriarchs. Comp. Gieseler, i. 2, p. 192, not. 20, and p. 228, not. 72; and Hefele, ii. 525 sq.

(114; original 522): Epist. 113, to Pope Leo I.

(115; original 523): That the apostle Andrew brought the gospel to the ancient Byzantium, is an entirely unreliable legend of later times.

(116; original 524): One exception is the brief pontificate of the Arian, Felix II, whom the emperor Constantius, in 355, forcibly enthroned during the exile of Liberius, and who is regarded by some as an illegitimate anti-pope. The accounts respecting him are, however, very conflicting, and so are the opinions of even Roman Catholic historians. Liberius also, in 357, lapsed for a short time into Arianism that he might be recalled from exile. Another and later exception is Pope Honorius, whom even the sixth ecumenical council of Constantinople, 681, anathematized for Monothelite heresy.

(117; original 525): Recall the interpolations of papistic passages in the works of Cyprian; the Roman enlargement of the sixth canon of Nice; the citation of the Sardican canon under the name and the authority of the Nicene council; and the later notorious pseudo-Isidorian decretals. The popes, to be sure, were not the original authors of these falsifications, but they used them freely and repeatedly for their purposes.

(118; original 526): Concil. Nicaean. of 325, can. 6, in the Latin version of Rufinus (Hist. Eccl. x. 6): "Et ut apud Alexandria et in urbe Roma vetusta consuetudo servetur, ut vel ille Ægypti, vel hic suburbicariarum ecclesiarum sollicitudinem gerat." The words suburb. eccl. are wanting in the Greek original, and are a Latin definition of the patriarchal diocese of Rome at the end of the fourth century. Since the seventeenth century they have given rise to a long controversy among the learned. The jurist Gothofredus and his friend Salmasius limited the regiones suburbicariae to the small province of the Praefectus Urbis, i.e. to the city of Rome with the immediate vicinity to the hundredth milestone; while the Jesuit Sirmond extended it to the much greater official district of the Vicarius Urbis, viz., the ten provinces of Campania, Tuscia with Umbria, Picenum suburbicarium, Valeria, Samnium, Apulia with Calabria, Lucania and Brutii, Sicilia, Sardinia, and Corsica. The comparison of the Roman bishop with the Alexandrian in the sixth canon of the Nicene council favors the latter view; since even the Alexandrian diocese likewise stretched over several provinces. The Prisca, however—a Latin collection of canons from the middle of the fifth century—has perhaps hit the truth of the matter, in saying, in its translation of the canon in question: "Antiqui moris est ut urbis Romae episcopus habeat principatum, ut suburbicaria loca [i.e. here, no doubt, the smaller province of the Praefectus] et omnem provinciam suam [i.e. the larger district of the Vicarius, or a still wider, indefinite extent] sollicitudine sua gubernet." Comp. Mansi, Coll. Conc. vi. 1127, and Hefele, i. 380 sqq.

(119; original 527): According to the political division of the empire, the Roman patriarchate embraced in the fifth century three praefectures, which were divided into eight political dioceses and sixty-nine provinces. These are, (1) the praefecture of Italy, with the three dioceses of Italy, Illyricum, and Africa; (2) the praefectum Galliarum, with the dioceses of Gaul, Spain, and Britain; (3) the praefecture of Illyricum (not to be confounded with the province of Illyria, which belonged to the praefecture of Italy), which, after 879, was separated indeed from the Western empire, as Illyricum orientale, but remained ecclesiastically connected with Rome, and embraced the two dioceses of Macedonia and Dacia. Comp. Wiltsch, l.c. i. 67 sqq.; Maassen, p. 125; and Hefele, i. 383.

(120; original 528): Contra Julianum, lib. i. cap. 6.

(121; original 529): Epistola decretales; an expression, which, according to Gieseler and others, occurs first about 500, in the so-called decretum Gelasii de libris recipiendis et non recipiendis.

(122; original 530): See the information concerning the conferring of the pallium in Wiltsch, i. 68 sq.

(123; original 531): This is conceded by Hefele, i. 383 sq.: "It is, however, not to be mistaken, that the bishop of Rome did not everywhere, in all the West, exercise full patriarchal rights; that, to wit, in several provinces, simple bishops were ordained without his coöperation." And not only simple bishops, but also metropolitans. See the text.

(124; original 532): Aujtokevfaloi, also ajkevfaloi, as in the East especially the archbishops of Cyprus and Bulgaria were called, and some other metropolitans, who were subject to no patriarch.

(125; original 533): Comp. Wiltsch, i. 234.

(126; original 534): Comp. Gregory I., Epist. l. iv. 49; and Wiltsch, i. 236 sq. To the metropolis of Aquileia belonged the bishopric of Verona, Tridentum (the Trent, since become so famous), Aemona, Altinum, Torcellum, Pola, Celina, Sabiona, Forum Julii, Bellunum, Concordia, Feltria, Tarvisium, and Vicentia.

(127; original 535): Baron. Ann. ad ann. 433; Wiltsch, i. 69, 87.

(128; original 536): Comp. the relevant Acts of councils in Gieseler, i. 2, p. 221 sqq., and an extended description of this case of appeal in Greenwood, Cath. Petri, i. p. 299-310, and in Hefele, Concilien-Gesch. ii. 107 sqq., 120, 123 sq.

(129; original 537): Mansi, iii. 839 sq.

(130; original 538): Epist. 87; Mansi, vi. 120.

(131; original 539): Ep. 93 and 95; Mansi, vi. 131 and 132.

(132; original 540): Mansi, vii. 972.

(133; original 541): Greg. Ep. i. 41; Mansi, ix. 1059. Comp. Wiltsch, i. 71.

(134; original 542): This difference shows itself in the two editions of the works of Leo the Great, respectively: that of the French Pasquier Quesnel, a Gallican and Jansenist (exiled 1681, died at Brussels 1719), which also contains the works, and a vindication, of Hilary of Arles (Par. 1675, in 2 vols.), and was condemned in 1676 by the Congregation of the Index, without their even reading it; and that of the two brothers Ballerini, which appeared in opposition to the former (Ven. 1755-1757, 3 vols.), and represents the Italian ultramontane side. Comp. further on this contest of Hilarius Arelatensis (not to be confounded with Hilarius Pictaviensis, Hilarius Narbonensis, and others of the same name) with Pope Leo, the Vita Hilarii of Honoratus Massiliensis, of about the year 490 (printed in Mansi, vi. 461 sqq., and in the Acta Sanct. ad d. 5 Maji); the article by Perthel, in Illgen’s Zeitschrift for Hist. Theol. 1843; Greenwood, l.c. i. p. 350-356; Milman, Lat. Christianity, i. p. 269-276 (Amer. ed.); and the article "Hilarius" in Wetzer and Welte’s Kirchenlexic vol. v. p. 181 sqq.

(135; original 543): "Nisi magnitudo causae etiam nostrum exquirat examen." Gieseler, i. 2, p. 218; Greenwood, i. p. 299.

(136; original 544): Comp. Bonifacii I Epist. 12 ad Hilarium Narbon. (not Arelatensen), a.d. 422, in Gieseler, p. 219. Boniface here speaks in favor of the Nicene principle, that each metropolitan should rule simply over one province. Greenwood overlooks this change, and hence fully justifies Hilary on the ground of the appointment of Zosimus. But even though this appointment had stood, the deposition of a bishop was still a causa major, which Hilary, as vicar of the pope, should have laid before him for ratification.

(137; original 545): Leo, Epist. 10 (al. 89) ad Episc. provinciae Viennensis. What an awful perversion this of the true Christian stand-point!

(138; original 546): The popes Vigil, 539-555, Pelagius, 555-559, and Gregory the Great conferred on the archbishop of Arles, besides the pallium, also the papal vicariate (vices). Comp. Wiltsch, i. 71 sq.

(139; original 547): At all events, no reconciliation can be certainly proved. Hilary did, indeed, according to the account of his disciple and biographer, who some forty years after his death encircled him with the halo, take some steps toward reconciliation, and sent two priests as delegates with a letter to the Roman prefect, Auxiliaris. The latter endeavored to act the mediator, but gave the delegates to understand, that Hilary, by his vehement boldness, had too deeply wounded the delicate ears of the Romans. In Leo’s letter a new trespass is charged upon Hilary, on the rights of the bishop Projectus, after the deposition of Celidonius. And Hilary died soon after this contest (449). Waterland ascribed to him the Athanasian Creed, though without good reason.

(140; original 548): Comp. Gieseler, i. 2, p. 21 5 sqq.; and Wiltsch, i. 72 sqq., 431 sqq.

(141; original 549): The name papa—according to some an abbreviation of pater patrum, but more probably, like the kindred abbas, pavppa", or pavpa", pa-pa, simply an imitation of the first prattling of children, thus equivalent to father—was, in the West, for a long time the honorary title of every bishop, as a spiritual father; but, after the fifth century, it became the special distinction of the patriarchs, and still later was assigned exclusively to the Roman bishop, and to him in an eminent sense, as father of the whole church. Comp. Du Cange, Glossar. s. verb. Papa and Pater Patrum; and Hoffmann, Lexic. univers. iv. p. 561. In the same exclusive sense the Italian and Spanish papa, the French pape, the English pope, and the German Papst or Pabst, are used. In the Greek and Russian churches, on the contrary, all priests are called Popes (from pavpa", papa). The titles apostolicus, vicarius Christi, summus pontifex, sedes apostolica, were for a considerable time given to various bishops and their sees, but subsequently claimed exclusively by the bishops of Rome.

(142; original 550): Matt. xvi. 18: Su; ei| Pevtro", kai; ejpi; tauvth/ th'/ pevtra/ [mark the change of the gender from the masculine to the feminine, from the person to the thing or the truth confessed—a change which disappears in the English and German versions] oijkodomhvsw mou th;n ejkklhsivan, kai; puvlai a{/dou ouj katiscuvsousin aujth'". Comp. the commentators, especially Meyer, Lange, Alford, Wordsworth, ad loc., and my Hist. of the Apost. Church, § 90 and 94 (N. Y. ed. p. 350 sqq., and 374 sqq.).

(146; original 554): So Chrysostom ad 2 Thess. ii. 7; Hieronymus, Ep. cxxi. qu. 11 (tom. i. p. 880 sq.); Augustine, De Civit. Dei, lib. xx. cap. 19.

(147; original 555): De schismate Donatistarum, lib. ii. cap. 2, 3, and l. vii. 3. The work was composed while Siricius was bishop of Rome, hence about 384.

(148; original 556): Ambr. Sermo ii. in festo Petri et Pauli: "In urbe Romae, quae principatum et caput obtinet nationum: scilicet ut ubi caput superstitionis erat, illic caput quiesceret sanctitatis, et ubi gentilium principes habitabant, illic ecclesiarum principes morerentur." In Ps. 40: "Ipse est Petrus cui dixit: Tu es Petrus ... ubi ergo Patrus, ibi ecclesia; ubi ecclesia, ibi mulla mors, sed vita eterna." Comp. the poetic passage in his Morning Hymn, in the citation from Augustine further on. But in another passage he likewise refers the rock to Christ, in Luc. ix. 20: "Petra est Christus," etc.

(149; original 557): De incarnat. Domini, c. 4: "Primatum confessionis utique, non honoris, primatum fidei, non ordinis."

(150; original 558): De Spiritu S. ii. 12: "Nec Paulus inferior Petro, quamvis ille ecclesiae fundamentum." Sermo ii. in festo P. et P., just before the above-quoted passage: "Ergo beati Petrus et Paulus eminent inter universos apostolos, et peculiari quadam praerogativa praecellunt. Verum inter ipsos, quis cui praeponatur, incertum est. Puto enim illos aequales esse meritis, qui aequales sunt passione." Augustine, too, once calls Paul, not Peter, caput et princeps apostolorum, and in another place that he tanti apostolatus meruit principatum.

(151; original 559): Hieron. in Amos, vi. 12: "Petra Christus est qui donavit apostolis suis, ut ipsi quoque petrae vocentur." And in another place: "Ecclesia Catholica super Petram Christum stabili radici fundata est."

(152; original 560): Adv. Jovin. l. i. cap. 26 (in Vallars. ed., tom. ii. 279), in reply to Jovinian’s appeal to Peter in favor of marriage: "At dicis: super Petrum fundatur ecclesia; licet id ipsum in alio loco super omnes apostolos fiat, et cuncti claves regni coelorum accipiant, et ex aequo super eos fortitudo ecclesiae solidetur, tamen propterea inter duodecim unus eligitur, ut capite constituto, schismatis tollatur occasio." So Epist. xv. ad Damasum papam (ed. Vall. i. 37).

(153; original 561): Comp. Epist. 146, ed. Vall. i. 1076 (or Ep. 101 ed. Bened., al. 85) ad Evangelum: "Ubicunque fuerit episcopus, sive Romae, sive Eugubii, sive Constantinopoli, sive Rhegii, sive Alexandriae, sive Tanis [an intentional collocation of the most powerful and most obscure bishoprics], ejusdem est meriti, ejusdem est et sacerdotii. Potentia divitiarum et paupertatis humilitas vel sublimiorem vel inferiorem episcopum non facit. Caeterum omnes apostolorum successores sunt."

(154; original 562): Comp. § 52, above. J. Craigie Robertson, Hist. of the Christian Church to 590 (Lond. 1854), p. 286, note, finds a remarkable negative evidence against the papal claims in St. Jerome’s Ep. 125, "where submission to one head is enforced on monks by the instinctive habits of beasts, bees, and cranes, the contentions of Esau and Jacob, of Romulus and Remus, the oneness of an emperor in his dominions, of a judge in his province, of a master in his house, of a pilot in a ship, of a general in an army, of a bishop, the archpresbyter, and the archdeacon in a church; but there is no mention of the one universal bishop."

(155; original 563): Ep. xv. (alias 57) ad Damasum papam (ed. Vall. l. 37 sq.): "Facessat invidia: Romani culminis recedat ambitio, cum successore piscatoris et discipulo crucis loquor. Ego nullum primum, nisi Christum sequens, Beatitudini tuae, id est cathedrae Petri, communione consocior. Super illam petram aedificatam ecclesiam scio. Quicunque extra hanc domum agnum comederit, profanus est. Si quis in Noe arca non fuerit, peribit regnante diluvio."

(156; original 564): Hier. Com. in Ep. ad Galat. ii. 11, 12 (ed. Vallars. tom. vii. col. 409): "Non quod aliud significat Petrus, aliudCephas, sed quo quam nos Latine et Graece petram vocemus, hanc Hebraei et Syri, propter linguae inter se viciniam, Cephan, nuncupent."

(157; original 565): Retract. l. i. c. 21.

(158; original 566): In the Ambrosian Morning Hymn: "Aeterne rerum conditor."

(159; original 567): Tract. in Evang. Joannis, 124, § 5. The original is quoted among others by Dr. Gieseler, i. 2, p. 210 (4th ed.), but with a few unessential omissions.

(160; original 568): Especially by Calov in the Lutheran church, and quite recently by Dr. Wordsworth in the Church of England (Commentary on Matt. xvi. 18). But Dr. Alford decidedly protests against it, with most of the modern commentators.

(161; original 569): De utilit. credendi, § 35, he traces the development of the church "ab apostolica sede per successiones apostolorum;" and Epist. 43, he incidentally speaks of the "Romana ecclesia in qua semper apostolicae cathedrae viguit principatus." Greenwood, i. 296 sq., thus resolves the apparent contradiction in Augustine: "In common with the age in which he lived, he (St. Augustine) was himself possessed with the idea of a visible representative unity, and considered that unity as equally the subject of divine precept and institution with the church-spiritual itself. The spiritual unity might therefore stand upon the faith of Peter, while the outward and visible oneness was inherent in his person; so that while the church derived her esoteric and spiritual character from the faith which Peter had confessed, she received her external or executive powers from Peter through ’the succession of bishops’ sitting in Peter’s chair. Practically, indeed, there was little to choose between the two theories." Comp. also the thorough exhibition of the Augustinian theory of the Catholic church and her attributes by Dr. Rothe, in his work Die Anfänge der christlichen Kirche, i. p. 679-711.

(162; original 570): De diversis Serm. 108: Has enim claves non homo unus, sed unitas accepit ecclesiae. Hinc ergo Petri excellentia praedicatur, quia ipsius universitatis et unitatis figuram gessit quando ei dictum est: tibi trado, quod omnibus traditum est, etc.

Eminent inter universos apostlos et peculiari quadam praerogativa praecellunt. Verum inter ipsos quis cui praeponatur, incertum est." The same sentence in Ambrose, De Spir. S. ii. 12.

The last sentence (ajlla; prosecwrhvsamen th'/' basilivdi Rwvmh/) is by some regarded as a later interpolation in favor of the papacy. But it contains no concession of superiority. Chrysostom immediately goes on to say: "We have indeed not retained the body of Peter, but we have retained the faith of Peter; and while we retain his faith, we have himself."

(170; original 578): That this is the true date appears from the recently discovered Festival Epistles of Athanasius, published in Syriac by Cureton (London, 1848), in an English translation by Williams (Oxford, 1854), and in German by Larsow (Leipzig, 1852). Mansi puts the council in the year 344, but most writers, including Gieseler, Neander, Milman, and Greenwood, following the erroneous statement of Socrates (ii. 20) and Sozomen (iii. 12), place it in the year 347. Comp. on the subject Larsow, Die Festbriefe des Athanasius, p. 31; and Hefele, Conciliengesch. i. p. 513 sqq.

(171; original 579): Can. 3, 4, and 5 (in the Latin translation, can. 3, 4, and 7), in Mansi, iii. 23 sq., and in Hefele, i. 539 sqq., where the Greek and the Latin Dionysian text is given with learned explanations. The Greek and Latin texts differ in some points.

(172; original 580): So the much discussed canones are explained not only by Protestant historians, but also by Catholic of the Gallican school, like Peter de Marca, Quesnel, Du-Pin, Richer, Febronius. This interpretation agrees best with the whole connection; with the express mention of Julius (which is lacking indeed, in the Latin translation of Prisca and in Isidore, but stands distinctly in the Greek and Dionysian texts: jIoulivw/ tw'/ ejpiskovpw/ JRwvmh",Julio Romano episcopo); with the words, " Si vobis placet" (can. 3), whereby the appeal in question is made dependent first on the decree of this council; and finally, with the words, "Sancti Petri apostoli memoriam honoremus," which represent the Roman bishop’s right of review as an honorary matter. What Hefele urges against these arguments (i. 548 sq.), seems to me very insufficient.

(173; original 581): Baronius, Natalis Alexander, and Mansi have endeavored indeed to establish for the council an ecumenical character, but in opposition to the weightiest ancient and modern authorities of the Catholic church. Comp. Hefele, i. 596 sqq,

(174; original 582): It is also to be observed, that the synodal letters, as well as the orthodox ecclesiastical writers of this and the succeeding age, which take notice of this council, like Socrates, Sozomen, Theodoret, and Basil, make no mention of those decrees concerning Rome.

(175; original 583): Comp. § 56.

(176; original 584): Ep. ad Conc. Cartha. and Ep. ad Concil. Milev., both in 416. In reference to this decision, which went against Pelagius, Augustine uttered the word so often quoted by Roman divines: "Causa finita est; utinam aliquando finiatur error." But when Zosimus, the successor of Innocent, took the part of Pelagius, Augustine and the African church boldly opposed him, and made use of the Cyprianic right of protest."Circumstances alter cases."

(177; original 585): As Quesnel and most of his successors infer from Prosper’s Chronicle, and a passage in Leo’s Ep. 31, c. 4, where he assigns among the reasons for not attending the council at Ephesus in 449, that he could not "deserere patriam et sedem apostolicam." Patria, however, may as well mean Italy, or at least the diocese of Rome, including the ten suburbican provinces. In the Liber pontificalis he is called "natione Tuscus," but in two manuscript copies, "natione Romanus." Canisius, in the Acta Sanctorum, adopts the former view. Butler reconciles the difficulty by supposing that he was descended of a noble Tuscan family, but born at Rome.

(178; original 586): Ep. v. ad Episcopos Metrop. per Illyricum constitutos, c. 2 (ed. Ball. i. 617, in Migne’s Patristic Libr. vol. liv. p. 515): "Quia per omnes ecclesias cura nostra distenditur, exigente hoc a nobis Domino, qui apostolicae dignitatis beatissimo apostolo Petro primatum fidei suae remuneratione commisit, universalem ecclesiam in fundamenti ipsius [Quesnel proposes istius for ipsius] soliditate constituens, necessitatem sollicitudinis quam habemus, cum his qui nobis collegii caritate juncti sunt, sociamus."

(179; original 587): These views Leo repeatedly expresses in his sermons on the festival of St. Peter and on the anniversary of his own elevation, as well as in his official letters to the African, Illyrian, and South Gallic bishops, to Dioscurus of Alexandria, to the patriarch Anatolius of Constantinople, to the emperor Marcian and the empress Pulcheria. Particular proof passages are unnecessary. Comp. especially Ep. x., xi., xii., xiv., civ.-cvi. (ed. Baller.), and Perthel, l.c. p. 226-241, where the chief passages are given in full.

(180; original 588): "Cujus dignitas etiam in indigno haerede non deficit," Sermo iii. in Natal, ordin. c. 4 (vol. i. p. 13, ed. Ball.)."Etsi necessarium est trepidare de merito, religiosum est tamen gaudere de dono: quoniam qui mihi oneris est auctor, ipse est administrationis adjutor." Serm. ii. c. 1.

(181; original 589): Pet. v. 3.

(182; original 590): Gal. ii. 11.

Petro) quisquis principatum aestimat denegandum, illius quidem nullo modo potest minuere dignitatem; sed inflatus spiritu superbiae suae semetipsum in inferna demergit." Comp. (184; original 592): Comp. above, § 59.

(185; original 593): See the particulars in § 36, above, near the close

(187; original 595): Leo himself says nothing of his mission to Attila. Prosper, in Chron. ad ann. 452, mentions it briefly, and Canisius, in the Vita Leonis (in the Acta Sanctorum, for the month of April, tom. ii. p. 18), with later exaggerations.

(188; original 596): Comp. Leo’s 84th Sermon, which was preached soon after the departure of the Vandals, and Prosper, Chron ad ann. 455

(189; original 597): The Roman calendar places his name on the 11th of April. But different writers fix his death on June 28, Oct. 30 (Quesnel), Nov. 4 (Pagi), Nov. 10 (Butler). Butler quotes the concession of Bower, the apostate Jesuit, who, in his Lives of the Popes, says of Leo, that "he was without doubt a man of extraordinary parts, far superior to all who had governed that church before him, and scarce equalled by any since."

(190; original 598): Sermones de natali. Canisius (in Acta Sanct., l.c. p. 17) calls Leo Christianum Demosthenem.

(191; original 599): De vocatione omnium gentium—a work praised highly even by Erasmus, Luther, Bullinger, and Grotius. Quesnel has only proved the possibility of Leo’s being the author. Comp. Perthel, l.c. p. 127 sqq. The Sacramentarium Leonis, or a collection of liturgical prayers for all the festival days of the year, contains some of his prayers, but also many which are of a later date.

(192; original 600): This was the fifth (al. fourth) council under, Symmachus, held in Nov. 502, therefore later than the synodus palmaris. Comp. Hefele, ii. p. 625 sq.

(193; original 601): . Dante puts him in hell, and Baronius ascribes his sudden death to an evident judgment of God.

(194; original 602): Comp. Hefele, ii. p. 615 sqq.

(195; original 603): So named from the building in Rome, in which it was held: "A porticu beati Petri Apostoli, quae appellatur ad Palmaria," as Anastasius says. In the histories of councils it is erroneously given as Synodus III. Many historians, Gieseler among them, place it in the year 503.

(196; original 604): Libellus apologeticus pro Synodo IV. Romana, in Mansi, viii. 274. This vindication was solemnly adopted by the sixth Roman council under Symmachus, in 503, and made equivalent to a decree of council.

(197; original 605): Even Justinian repeatedly applied to the patriarch of Constantinople officially the title oijkomeniko;" patriavrch", universalis patriarcha.

(198; original 606): Bellarmine disposes of this apparent testimony of one of the greatest and best popes against the system of popery, which has frequently been urged since Calvin by Protestant controversialists, by assuming that the term episcopus universalis is used in two very different senses."Respondeo," he says (in his great controversial work, De controversiis christianae fidei, etc., de Romano pontifice, lib. ii. cap. 31), "duobus modis posse intelligi nomen universalis episcopi. Uno modo, ut ille, qui dicitur universalis, intelligatur esse solus episcopus omnium urbium Christianarum, ita ut caeteri non sint episcopi, sed vicarii tantum illius, qui dicitur episcopus universalis, et hoc modo nomen hoc est vere profanum, sacrilegum et antichristianum.... Altero modo dici potest episcopus universalis, qui habet curam totius ecclesiae, sed generalem, ita ut non excludat particulares episcopos. Et hoc modo nomen hoc posse tribui Romano pontifici ex mente Gregorii probatur."

(199; original 607): The name suvnodo" oijkoumenikhv (concilium universale, s. generale) occurs first in the sixth canon of the council of Constantinople in 381. The oijkoumevnh (sc. gh') is, properly, the whole inhabited earth; then, in a narrower sense, the earth inhabited by Greeks, in distinction from the barbarian countries; finally, with the Romans, the orbis Romanus, the political limits of which coincided with those of the ancient Graeco-Latin church. But as the bishops of the barbarians outside the empire were admitted, the ecumenical councils represented the entire Catholic Christian world.

(200; original 608): Acts xv., and Gal. ii. Comp. my History of the Apostolic Church, §§ 67-69 (Engl. ed., p. 245-257). Mansi, l.c. tom. i. p. 22 (De quadruplici Synodo Apostolorum), and other Roman Catholic writers, speak of four Apostolic Synods: Acts i. 13 sqq., for the election of an apostle; ch. vi. for the election of deacons; ch. xv. for the settlement of the question of the binding authority of the law of Moses; and ch. xxi. for a similar object. But we should distinguish between a private conference and consultation, and a public synod.

(201; original 609): It is usually supposed there were only four or five different kinds of council. But Hefele reckons eight (i. p. 3 and 4) adding to those above named the irregularsuvnodoiejndhmou'sai, also the synods of the bishops of two or more provinces finally the concilia mixta, consisting of the secular and spiritual dignitaries province, as separate classes.

(202; original 610): A similar order, with different times, appears still earlier in the 37th of the apostolic canons, where it is said (in the ed. of Ueltzen, p. 244):Deuvterontou'e[tou" suvnodo" genevsqwtw'nejpiskovpwn.

(203; original 611): This Sunday, the celebration of which was ordered by the empress Theodora in 842, is called among the Greeks the kuriakhvth'" ojrqodoxiva". On that day the ancient councils are dramatically reproduced in the public worship.

(204; original 612): The schismatical Donatists alone held a council at Carthage in 308, of two hundred and seventy bishops (Comp. Wiltsch, Kirchl. Geogr. u. Statistik, i. p. 53 and 54); while the second ecumenical council numbered only a hundred and fifty, the third a hundred and sixty (a hundred and ninety-eight), and the fifth a hundred and sixty-four.

(205; original 613): Schröckh says (vol. viii. p. 201), unjustly, that this general consent belongs among the "empty conceits." Of course the unanimity must be limited to orthodox Christendom.

(206; original 614): This is conceded even by the Roman Catholic church historian Hefele (i. p. 7), in opposition to, Bellarmine and other Romish divines."The first eight general councils," says he, "were appointed and convoked by the emperors; all the subsequent councils, on the contrary [i.e. all the Roman Catholic general councils], by the popes; but even in those first councils there appears a certain participation of the popes in their convocation, more or less prominent in particular instances." The latter assertion is too sweeping, and can by no means be verified in the history of the first two of these councils, nor of the fifth.

(207; original 615): As regards the council of Nicaea: according to Eusebius and all the ancient authorities, it was called by Constantine alone; and not till three centuries later, at the council of 680, was it claimed that Pope Sylvester had any share in the convocation. As to the council of Constantinople in 381: the Roman theory, that Pope Damasus summoned it in conjunction with Theodosius, rests on a confusion of this council with another and an unimportant one of 382. Comp. the notes of Valesius to Theodoret, Hist. Ecel. v. 9; and Hefele (who here himself corrects his earlier view), vol. i. p. 8, and vol. ii. p. 36.

(208; original 616): Euseb., Vita Const. iii. 15: Cristou'basileiva" e[doxena[nti" fantasiou'sqaieijkovna, o[nartjei|naiajlljoujcu{parto;ginovmenon.

(209; original 617): Mansi, vii. 170 sqq. The emperor is called there not simply divine, which would be idolatrous enough, but most divine, oJqeiovtato": kai;eujsebevstato" hJmw'ndespovth", divinissimus et piissimus noster imperator ad sanctam synodum dixit, etc. And these adulatory epithets occur repeatedly in the acts of this council.

(213; original 621): ]Edoxetw'/ pneuvmatiaJgivw/ kai;hJmi'n, Acts xv. 28. The provincial councils, too, had already used this phrase; e.g. the Concil. Carthaginiense, of 252 (in the Opera Cypriani): "Placuit nobis, Sancto Spiritu suggerente, et Domino per visiones multas et manifestas admonente." So the council of Arles, in 314: "Placuit ergo, presente Spiritu Sancto et angelis ejus."

(214; original 622): Qeivanejntolhvn, andqeivanbouvlhsin, in Euseb., Vita Const. iii. 20. Comp. his Ep. ad Eccl. Alexandr., in Socrates, H. E. i. 9 where he uses similar expressions.

(215; original 623): Isidore of Pelusium also styles the Nicene council divinely inspired, qeovqenejmpneu"qei'sa (Ep. 1. iv. Ep. 99). So Basil the Great, Ep. 114 (in the Benedictine edition of his Opera omnia, tom. iii. p. 207), where he says that the 318 fathers of Nice have not spoken without the ejnevrgeiatou'aJgivoupneuvmato" (non sine Spiritus Sancti afflatu).

(216; original 624): Act. i., in Mansi, vi. p. 672. We quote from the Latin translation: "Nullo autem modo patimur a quibusdam concuti definitam fidem, sive fidei symbolum, a sanctis patribus nostris qui apud Nicaeam convenerunt illis temporibus: nec permittimus aut nobis, aut aliis, mutare aliquod verbum ex his quae ibidem continentur, aut unam syllabam praeterire, memores dicentis: Ne transferas terminos aeternos, quos posuerunt patres tui (Prov. xxii. 8; Matt. x. 20). Non enim erant ipsi loquentes, sed ipse Spiritus Dei et Patris qui procedit ex ipso."

(217; original 625): jJOblasfhmhqei;" parjaujtou'kuvrio" jIh". Cristo;" w{risedia;th'" parouvsh" aJgiwtavth" sunovdou.

(218; original 626): Lib. i. Ep. 25 (ad Joannem episcopum Constant., et caeteros patriarchas, in Migne’s edition of Gr. Opera, tom. iii. p. 478, or in the Bened. ed. iii. 515): "Praeterea, quia corde creditur ad justitiam, ore autem confessio fit ad salutem, sicut sancti evangelii quatuor libros, sic quatuor concilia suscipere et venerari me fateor. Nicaenum scilicet in quo perversum Arii dogma destruitur; Constantinopolitanum quoque, in quo Eunomii et Macedonii error convincitur; Ephesinum etiam primum, in quo Nestorii impietas judicatur; Chalcedonense vero, in quo Eutychetii [Eutychis] Dioscorique pravitas reprobatur, tota devotione complector, integerrima approbatione custodio: quia in his velut in quadrato lapide, sanctae fidei structura consurgit, et cujuslibet vitae atque actionis existat, quisquis eorum soliditatem non tenet, etiam si lapis esse cernitur, tamen extra aedificium jacet. Quintum quoque concilium pariter veneror, in quo et epistola, quae Ibae dicitur, erroris plena, reprobatur," etc.

(219; original 627): Justin. Novell. cxxxi."Quatuor synodorum dogmata sicut sanctas scripturas accipimus, et regulas sicut leges observamus."

(220; original 628): Conc. Nic. can. 15: }Wsteajpo;povlew" eij" povlinmh;metabaivneinmhvteejpivskoponmhvtepresbuvteronmhvtediavkonon. This prohibition arose from the theory of the relation between a clergyman and his congregation, as a mystical marriage, and was designed to restrain clerical ambition. It appears in the Can. Apost. 13, 14, but was often violated. At the Nicene council itself there were several bishops, like Eusebius of Nicomedia, and Eustathius of Antioch, who had exchanged their first bishopric for another and a better.

(221; original 629): Novmou" pavlaiteqnhkovta", Carm. de vita sua, v. 1810.

(222; original 630): Epist. 106 (al. 80) ad Anatolium, and Epist. 105 ad Pulcheriam. Comp. above, § 57. Even Gregory I., so late as 600, writes in reference to the canones of the Constantinopolitan council of 381: "Romana autem ecclesia eosdem canones vel gesta Synodi illius hactenus non habet, nec accepit; in hoc autem eam accepit, quod est per eam contra Macedonium definitum." Lib. vii. Ep. 34, ad Eulogium episcopum Alexandr. (tom. iii. p. 882, ed. Bened., and in Migne’s ed., iii. 893.)

(223; original 631): De Baptismo contra Donatistas, I. ii. 3 (in the Benedictine edition of August. Opera, tom. ix. p. 98): "Quis autem nesciat, sanctam Scripturam canonicam, tam Veteris quam Novi Testamenti, certis suis terminis contineri, eamque omnibus posterioribus Episcoporum literis ita praeponi, ut de illa omnino dubitari et disceptari non possit, utrum verum vel utrum rectum sit, quidquid in ea scriptum esse constiterit; Episcoporum autem literas quae post confirmatum canonem vel scripta sunt vel scribuntur, et per sermonem forte sapientiorem cujuslibet in ea re peritioris, et per aliorum Episcoporum graviorem auctoritatem doctioremque prudentiam, et per concilia licere reprehendi, si quid in eis forte a veritate deviatum est; et ipsa concilia, quae per singulas regiones vel provincias fiunt, plenariorum conciliorum auctoritate, quae fiunt ex universo orbe Christiano, sine ullis ambagibus cedere; ipsaque plenaria saepe priora posterioribus emendari, quum aliquo experimento rerum aperitur quod clausum erat et cognoscitur quod latebat; sine ullo typho sacrilegae superbiae, sine ulla inflata cervice arrogantiae, sine ulla contentione lividae invidiae, cum sancta humilitate, cum pace catholica, cum caritate christiana." Comp. the passage Contra Maximinum Arianum, ii. cap. 14, § 3 (in the Bened. ed., tom. viii. p. 704), where he will have even the decision of the Nicene council concerning the homousion measured by the higher standard of the Scriptures.

(224; original 632): Contra Epistolam Manichaei, lib. i. c. 5 (in the Bened. ed., tom. viii. p. 154): "Ego vero evangelio non crederem, nisi me ecclesiae catholicae commoveret auctoritas."

(225; original 633): Commonitorium, c. 23 (in Migne’s Curs. Patrol. tom. 50, p. 667): "Sed forsitan dicit aliquis: Nullusne ergo in ecclesia Christi profectus habebitur religionis? Habeatur plane et maximus .... Sed ita tamen ut vere profectus sit ille fidei, non permutatio. Si quidem ad profectum pertinet ut in semetipsum unaquaeque res amplificetur; ad permutationem vero, ut aliquid ex alio in aliud transvertatur. Crescat igitur oportet et multum vehementerque proficiat tam singulorum quam omnium, tam unius hominis, quam totius ecclesiae, aetatum ac seculorum gradibus, intelligentia, scientia, sapientia, sed in suo dutaxat genere, in eodem scilicet dogmate, eodem sensu, eademque sententia."

(226; original 634): Acts xv. 6: Pollh'" suzhthvsew" genomevnh"; which Luther indeed renders quite too strongly: " After they had wrangled long." The English versions from Tyndale to King James translate: " much disputing."

(227; original 635): Hist. Eccl. lib. v. cap. 7.

(228; original 636): Ep. ad Procop. 55, old order (al. 130). Similar representations occur in Ep. 76, 84; Carm. de vita sua, v. 1680-1688; Carm. x. v. 92; Carm. Adv. Episc. v. 154. Comp. Ullmann, Gregor. von Naz., p. 246 sqq., and p. 270. It is remarkable that Gibbon makes no use of these passages to support his summary judgment of the general councils at the end of his twentieth chapter, where he says: "The progress of time and superstition erased the memory of the weakness, the passion, the ignorance, which disgraced these ecclesiastical synods; and the Catholic world has unanimously submitted to the infallible decrees of the general councils."

(229; original 637): jEkbohvsei" dhmotikaiv. See Harduin, tom. ii. p. 71 sqq., and Mansi, tom. vi. p. 590 sq. Comp. also Hefele, ii. p. 406 sq.

(230; original 638): This is the usual estimate, resting on the authority of Athanasius, Basil (Ep. 114; Opera, t. iii. p 207, ed. Bened.), Socrates, Sozomen, and Theodoret; whence the council is sometimes called the Assembly of the Three Hundred and Eighteen. Other data reduce the number to three hundred, or to two hundred and seventy, or two hundred and fifty, or two hundred and eighteen; while later tradition swells it to two thousand or more.

(231; original 639): For some time the Egyptian and Syrian churches commemorated the council of Nicaea by an annual festival.

(232; original 640): The opposition council, which John of Antioch, on his subsequent arrival, held in the same city in the cause of Nestorius and under the protection of the imperial commissioner Candidian, numbered forty-three members, and excommunicated Cyril, as Cyril had excommunicated Nestorius.

(233; original 641): The synod itself, in a letter to Leo, states the number as only five hundred and twenty; Leo, on the contrary (Ep. 102), speaks of about six hundred members; and the usual opinion (Tillemont, Memoires, t. xv. p. 641) raises the whole number of members, including deputies, to six hundred and thirty.

(234; original 642): Tria capitula, Kefavleia.

(235; original 643): The condemnation of a departed pope as a heretic by an ecumenical council is so inconsistent with the claims of papal infallibility, that Romish historians have tried their utmost to dispute the fact, or to weaken its force by sophistical pleading.

(236; original 644): Eij" Nivkaian. Nice and Nicene are properly misnomers, but sanctioned by the use of Gibbon and other great English writers.

(237; original 645): Trullum was a saloon with a cupola in the imperial palace of Constantinople.

(238; original 646): The Latins call it the fourth because they reject the fourth Constantinopolitan (the second Trullan) council of 692, because of its canons, and the fifth of 754 because it condemned the worship of images, which was subsequently sanctioned by the second council of Nicaea in 787.

(239; original 647): On the number of the ecumenical councils till that of Trent the Roman divines themselves are not agreed. The Gallicans reckon twenty-one, Bellarmine eighteen, Hefele only sixteen. The undisputed ones, besides the eight already mentioned Graeco-Latin councils, are these eight Latin: the first Lateran (Roman) council, a.d. 1123; the second Lateran, a.d. 1139; the third Lateran, a.d. 1179; the fourth Lateran, a.d. 1215); the first of Lyons, a.d. 1245; the second of Lyons, a.d. 1274; that of Florence, a.d. 1439; (the fifth Lateran, 1512-1517, is disputed;) and that of Trent, a.d. 1545-1563. The ecumenical character of the three reformatory councils of Pisa, Constance, and Basle, in the beginning of the fifteenth century, and of the fifth Lateran council, a.d. 1512-1517, is questioned among the Roman divines, and is differently viewed upon ultramontane and upon Gallican principles. Hefele considers them partially ecumenical; that is, so far as they were ratified by the pope. [But in the Revised edition of his Conciliengeschichte, 1873 sqq., he reckons twenty ecumenical councils, including the Vatican, 1870. See Appendix, p. 1032.]

(240; original 648): It is uncertain whether he obtained the surname Exiguus from his small stature or his monastic humility.

(241; original 649): It may be found in the above-cited Bibliotheca, vol. i., and in all good collections of councils. He says in the preface that, confusione priscae translationis (the Prisca or Itala) offensus, he has undertaken a new translation of the Greek canons.

(242; original 650): "Canones, qui dicuntur apostolorum, ... quibus plurimi consensum non praebuere facilem;" implying that Dionysius himself, with many others, doubted their apostolic origin. In a later collection of canons by Dionysius, of which only the preface remains, he entirely omitted the apostolic canons, with the remark: "Quos non admisit universitas, ego quoque in hoc opere praetermisi." On the pseudo-apostolic Canons and Constitutions, comp. vol. i. § 113 (p. 440-442), and the well-known critical work of the Roman Catholic theologian Drey.

(243; original 651): Suvntagmakanovnwn, Concordia canonum, in the Bibliotheca of Justellus, tom. ii.

Εἰ γὰρ ὀρθῶς ἐλέγχομεν ἄρτι, καὶ τῷ ὄντι θεοῖς μὲν ἄχρηστον ψεῦδος ἀνθρώποις δὲ χρήσιμον, ὡς ἐν φαρμάκου εἴδει, δῆλον ὅτι τὸ γε τοιοῦτον ἰατροῖς δοτέον, ἰδιώταις δὲ οὐχ ἁπτέον. Δῆλον, ἔφη. Τοῖς ἄρχουσι δὴ τῆς πόλεως, εἴπερ τισὶν ἄλλοις, προσήκει ψεύδεσθαι ἢ πολεμίων ἢ πολιτῶν ἕνεκα, ἐπ’ ὠφελείᾳ τῆς πόλεως· τοῖς δὲ ἄλλοις πᾶσιν οὐχ ἁπτέον τοῦ τοιούτου.   1. 例如，金口约翰 (Chrysostom)：《论祭司职》(De sacerdotio)；奥古斯丁 (Augustine)：《论基督教教义》(De doctrina Christiana)；哲罗姆 (Jerome)：若干书信；大贵格利 (Gregory the Great)：《牧职规程》(Regula pastoralis)。↩︎

  2. Ὡς ὁ μῦθος ποιεῖ τοὺς γίγαντας*（正如神话塑造巨人那样）。↩︎

  3. 贵格利 (Greg.)，《演说集》(Orat.) xliii，第26章（《全集》〔Opera omnia〕，本笃会版，巴黎，1842年，第i卷，第791页及下页）；其余演说及《论自己与反对主教们之诗》(Carmen de se ipse et advers. Episc.) 中也有类似段落。比较乌尔曼 (Ullmann)：《纳齐安的贵格利》(Greg. v. Naz.)，第511页及后。↩︎

  4. 《上帝之城》(De Civit. Dei)，第xx卷，第10章：“Erunt sacerdotes Dei et Christi et regnabunt cum eo mille annos (Apoc. xx. 6): non utique de solis episcopis et presbyteris dictum est, qui proprie jam vocantur in Ecclesia sacerdotes; sed sicut omnes Christianos dicimus propter mysticum chrisma, sic omnes sacerdotes, quoniam membra sunt unius sacerdotis. De quibus apostolus Petrus: Plebs, inquit, sancta regale sacerdotium (1 Pet. ii. 9).”（“他们必作上帝和基督的祭司，并与他一同作王一千年〔《启示录》xx. 6〕”：这当然不单指如今在教会中特称为祭司的主教和长老；正如我们因那奥秘的膏抹而称众人为基督徒，也同样称众人为祭司，因为他们都是那一位祭司的肢体。使徒彼得论到他们说：“圣洁的子民，有君尊的祭司”〔《彼得前书》ii. 9〕。）比较安布罗西亚斯特 (Ambrosiaster) 对《以弗所书》iv. 11的注释；哲罗姆对《提多书》i. 7的注释；以及教皇利奥一世 (Leo I)《讲道集》(Sermon.) iv. 1。↩︎

  5. 据罗马的革利免 (Clemens Romanus)《致哥林多人书》(ad Corinth.) 第44章，选立教会职员须经全会众同意，乃是使徒时期及使徒后时期的惯例；居普良 (Cyprian) 的书信，尤其是第68封，表明同一规则在第三世纪中叶仍然沿用。比较第i卷§105。↩︎

  6. Ζήτησις, ψήφισμα, ψῆφος*, scrutinium（审查）。↩︎

  7. Ἄξιος*，dignus（配得），或 ἀνάξιος*，indignus（不配）。《使徒宪章》(Constitut. Apost.) viii. 4；第二次阿尔勒会议 (Concil. Aurelat. ii.，公元452年)，第54条；纳齐安的贵格利 (Gregor. Naz.)《演说集》(Orat.) xxi。据亚历山大的彼得 (Peter of Alexandria) 的一封信，载狄奥多勒 (Theodoret)《教会史》(Hist. Eccl.) iv. 22，东方的主教是由 ἐπισκόπων συνόδῳ, ψήφῳ κληρικῶν, αἰτήσει λαῶν* 选立的。他本人遵照亚他那修 (Athanasius) 的意愿，“经教牧人员和城中首要人物一致同意”，被选为亚历山大大主教，继任亚他那修（公元373年；iv，第20章）。被逐之后，他指责其邪恶的继任者路基乌斯 (Lucius)，其中一项便是：“他用黄金买得主教职位，仿佛那是世俗的官爵……并没有按照教会的规章，由主教会议选立，由教牧人员投票选立，或应人民的请求而选立”（iv，第22章）。↩︎

  8. 《书信集》(Epist.) x，第4章（《著作集》，巴莱里尼版，第i卷，第637页）：“Expectarentur certe vota civium, testimonia populorum, quaereretur honoratorum arbitrium, electio clericorum ....”（当然应当等候市民表达意愿、人民提出见证，并征求显贵的判断和教牧人员的选举意见……。）同一封信第6章：Qui praefuturus est omnibus, ab omnibus eligatur.（将要治理众人的，应由众人选立。）↩︎

  9. 保利努斯 (Paulinus)，《安布罗斯传》(Vita Ambros.)；索佐门 (Sozomen)，《教会史》(H. E.) 第iv卷，第24章，及vii. 8。这位史家以上帝护理的特殊干预来为这种不合常规的做法辩解。↩︎

  10. 苏尔皮基乌斯·塞韦鲁斯 (Sulpitius Severus)，《马丁传》(Vita Mart.)，第7章：“Incredibilis multitudo non solum ex eo oppido [Tours], sed etiam ex vicinis urbibus ad suffragia ferenda convenerat,” 等（人数多得令人难以置信，不仅从那座城〔图尔〕，也从邻近各城聚集而来，参加投票）。↩︎

  11. 苏格拉底 (Socrates)，《教会史》(H. E.) vi. 2：Ψηφίσματι κοινῷ ὁμοῦ πάντων κλήρου τε φημὶ καὶ λαοῦ*..↩︎

  12. 《论祭司职》(De sacerdotio)，第iii卷，第15章。在同章稍后，他甚至说，许多人因其恶劣而获选，为的是防止他们落选后作恶：Οἱ δὲ, διὰ πονηρίαν, «εἰς τὴν τοῦ κλήρου καταλέγονται τάξιν», καὶ ἵνα μὴ, παροφθέντες, μεγάλα ἐργάσωνται κακά*。纳齐安的贵格利在其诗作 εἰς ἑαυτὸν καὶ περὶ ἐπισκόπων*，即《论自己与众主教》(De se ipso et de episcopis)，第330行及后（《著作集》，本笃会版，巴黎，第ii卷，第796页），以及其他地方所作的见证，与此完全相同。↩︎

  13. 索佐门 (Sozomenus)，《教会史》(Hist. Eccl.) vii，第8章。索佐门认为，这次选举中有上帝的特殊干预。↩︎

  14. 索佐门 (Sozomenus)，vii，第10章。除此之外，他和苏格拉底 (Socrates)《教会史》(H. E.) v，第8章，以及狄奥多勒 (Theodoret)《教会史》(H. E.) v，第8章，都对涅克塔里乌斯 (Nectarius) 的品格评价甚高。↩︎

  15. 787年在尼西亚举行的第七次大公会议，在其第三条教规中，基于对第一次尼西亚会议第四条教规的错误解释，明令禁止人民和世俗权力以任何方式参与主教选举。第八次大公会议也规定，主教只能由主教团选立。↩︎

  22. 参见苏格拉底 (Socrates)《教会史》(H. E.) i，第11章的记载，其中将禁止祭司结婚的提议称为一种新制，即 νόμος νεαρός*；又见索佐门 (Sozomen)《教会史》(H. E.) i，第23章；格拉修 (Gelasius)《尼西亚会议史》(Hist. Conc. Nic.) ii. 32。因此，这一记述有充分凭据，而且与东方教会的古老惯例以及《使徒宪章》和《使徒教规》的规定完全一致。尼西亚会议第三条教规并不与此抵触，因为它所禁止的只是 mulieres subintroductae（私下引入同居的女子）所涉及的不道德行为（比较第i卷§95）。几位罗马天主教神学家——巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius)、贝拉明 (Bellarmine)、瓦莱修斯 (Valesius)——极力想把教牧人员独身制追溯到使徒时代；他们的怀疑显然出于教义偏见，而罗马天主教史家赫费勒 (Hefele) 已在其《大公会议史》(Conciliengeschichte) 第i卷，第417页及后，给予充分的驳斥。↩︎

  24. 第5条教规（于尔岑〔Ueltzen〕版，第239页）：Ἐπίσκοπος ἢ πρεσβύτερος ἢ διάκονος τὴν ἑαυτοῦ γυναῖκα μὴ ἐκβαλλέτω προφάσει εὐλαβείας· ἐὰν δὲ ἐκβάλῃ, ἀφοριζέσθω, ἐπιμένων δὲ καθαιρείσθω*。比较《使徒宪章》(Const. Apost.) vi. 17。↩︎

  25. 他宣称：“上帝、法律和狄奥菲鲁斯 (Theophilus，亚历山大的主教) 那分别为圣的手，赐给了我一个妻子。我现在预先声明，并郑重申明，我决不会离弃她，也不会暗中与她同居，仿佛那是一种非法关系；因为前者完全违背宗教，后者则违背法律；我却盼望由她为我生育众多优秀的儿女”（《书信集》〔Epist.〕105，巴塞尔版〔ed. Basil.〕；吉泽勒引有希腊文原文）。关于已婚主教的事例，比较宾厄姆 (Bingham)，《基督教古制》(Christ. Antiq.)，第iv卷，第5章；J. A. 泰纳与A. 泰纳 (J. A. Theiner and A. Theiner)，《基督教教牧人员强制独身制的引入及其后果》(Die Einführung der erzwungenen Ehelosigkeit der christl. Geistlichen u. ihre Folgen)（阿尔滕堡，1828年），第i卷，第263页及后；以及吉泽勒 (Gieseler)，第i卷，第2分册，§97末尾各注。某些罗马天主教作者否认尼撒的贵格利 (Gregory of Nyssa) 与狄奥塞比娅 (Theosebia) 的婚姻，但纳齐安的贵格利《书信集》(Greg. Naz. Ep.) 95，以及尼撒的贵格利《论童贞》(Greg Nyss. De virg.) 3，似乎为此提供了充分的支持。↩︎

  29. 《致塔拉戈纳主教希梅里乌斯书》(Epist. ad Himerium Episc. Tarraconensem)，载阿尔杜安 (Harduin)，《会议文献》(Acta Conc.)，第i卷，第849–850页，第7章：“Hi vero, qui illiciti privilegii excusatione nituntur, ut sibi asserant veteri hoc lege concessum: noverint se ab omni ecclesiastico honore, quo indigne usi sunt, apostolicae sedis auctoritate dejectos .... Si quilibet episcopus, presbyter atque diaconus, quod non optamus, deinceps fuerit talis inventus, jam nunc sibi omnem per nos indulgentiae aditum intelligat obseratum: quia ferro necesse est excidantur vulnera, quae fomentorum non senserint medicinam.”（那些以不合法的特权为借口，声称古时律法准许他们如此行的人，应当知道，凭使徒教座的权柄，他们已被革除一切曾遭他们不配享用的教会尊荣……此后若发现任何主教、长老或执事仍如此行——这是我们所不愿的——他应当从现在起就明白，我们已将一切宽赦之门向他关闭；因为药敷既不能奏效的创伤，就必须用刀割除。）西里修斯 (Siricius) 的释经完全武断：他把成圣的要求（《利未记》xx. 7）仅限于祭司，并仅解释为禁绝夫妻房事；又把保罗关于随从肉体而行的话（《罗马书》viii. 8、9）解释为婚姻生活，仿佛婚姻因此就与圣洁的观念不相容。又比较格林伍德 (Greenwood)，《彼得教座》(Catheda Petri)，第i卷，第265页及下页，以及米尔曼 (Milman)，《拉丁基督教史》(Hist. of Latin Christianity)，第i卷，第119页（美国版），关于西里修斯的精辟评论。↩︎

  37. 《致瓦莱里乌斯书》第21封 (Epist. 21 ad Valerium)：“Nihil esse in hac vita et maxime hoc tempore facilius et laetitius et hominibus acceptabilius episcopi aut presbyteri aut diaconi officio, si perfunctorie atque adulatorie res agatur: sed nihil apud Deum miserius et tristius et damnabilius. Item nihil esse in hac vita et maxime hoc tempore difficilius, laboriosius, periculosius episcopi aut presbyteri aut diaconi officio, sed apud Deum nihil beatius, si eo modo militetur, quo noster imperator jubet.”（在今生，尤其在这个时代，若以敷衍和谄媚的态度行事，就没有什么比主教、长老或执事的职分更轻松、更愉快、更讨人喜欢；但在上帝面前，也没有什么比这更悲惨、更可悲、更当受定罪。同样，在今生，尤其在这个时代，没有什么比主教、长老或执事的职分更艰难、更劳苦、更危险；但若照我们统帅所吩咐的方式服役，在上帝面前就没有什么比这更有福。）此信写于他在公元391年受按立为长老后不久。见《著作集》(Opera)，本笃会版（ed. Bened.），第ii卷，第25页。↩︎

  38. 《演说集》(Orat.) xliii，第46章（《著作集》〔Opera〕，本笃会版〔ed. Bened.〕，第i卷，第791页），其拉丁译文为：“Nunc autem periculum est, ne ordo omnium sanctissimus, sit quoque omnium maxime ridiculus. Non enim virtute magis, quam maleficio et scelere, sacerdotium paratur; nec digniorum, sed potentiorum, throni sunt.”（如今的危险是，这一切职分中最神圣的职分，恐怕也成了一切职分中最可笑的。因为人取得祭司职分，与其说凭德行，不如说凭恶行和罪孽；宝座不属于更配得的人，而属于更有权势的人。）然而，在下一章，他却把自己的朋友巴西尔 (Basil) 描绘为一切美德的典范。↩︎

Vall.〕，第i卷，第252页及后）。不过，他也不放过修士，在《致尼波提安书》(ad Nepot.) 中说：“Nonnulli sunt ditiores monachi quam fuerant seculares et clerici qui possident opes sub Christo paupere, quas sub locuplete et fallaci Diabolo non habuerant.”（有些修士比他们在俗时更富有；有些教牧人员在贫穷的基督之下，竟拥有他们在富有而诡诈的魔鬼之下所不曾拥有的财富。）↩︎

Bipont.）：Εἰ γὰρ ὀρθῶς ἐλέγχομεν ἄρτι, καὶ τῷ ὄντι θεοῖς μὲν ἄχρηστον ψεῦδος ἀνθρώποις δὲ χρήσιμον, ὡς ἐν φαρμάκου εἴδει, δῆλον ὅτι τὸ γε τοιοῦτον ἰατροῖς δοτέον, ἰδιώταις δὲ οὐχ ἁπτέον. Δῆλον, ἔφη. Τοῖς ἄρχουσι δὴ τῆς πόλεως, εἴπερ τισὶν ἄλλοις, προσήκει ψεύδεσθαι ἢ πολεμίων ἢ πολιτῶν ἕνεκα, ἐπ’ ὠφελείᾳ τῆς πόλεως· τοῖς δὲ ἄλλοις πᾶσιν οὐχ ἁπτέον τοῦ τοιούτου.*。犹太哲学神学家斐洛 (Philo) 在其《论上帝不变》(Quod Deus sit immutabilis)，第302页，也持有类似看法。↩︎

  53. 连世俗官员也被排除在外，其理由见于402年罗马会议，该会议在第10条教规中规定：“身任世俗官职的人，因其职务几乎必然伴随的罪，若不先行补赎，便不能成为教牧人员。”比较曼西 (Mansi)，第iii卷，第1133页，以及赫费勒 (Hefele)，第ii卷，第75页。↩︎

  54. 比较赫费勒 (Hefele)，第ii卷，第574、638、686、687、753、760页等处的会议法令。甚至《使徒教规》(Can. Apost.) 第27、65、72条，也针对教牧人员中殴打、谋杀、偷盗等普通罪行；因此，这些罪行当时必已出现，因为立法总是着眼于实际情形。保罗的教牧书信中，并没有这类劝诫或禁令。↩︎

  55. 哲罗姆，《提多书注释》(Hieron. Comm. ad Tit.) i. 7：“Idem est ergo presbyter qui episcopus, et antequam diaboli instinctu studia in religione fierent ... communi presbyterorum consilio ecclesiae gubernabantur,” 等（因此，长老与主教原是同一职分；在魔鬼的煽动使宗教中产生党争以前……教会由长老共同议事治理）。比较《致长老埃万格卢斯书》(Epist. ad Evangelum presbyterum)（第146封，瓦拉尔西版〔ed. Vall.〕《著作集》〔Opera〕，第i卷，第1074页及后；本笃会版〔ed. Bened.〕第101封），以及《致奥凯阿努斯书》(Epist. ad Oceanum)（瓦拉尔西版第69封，本笃会版第82封）。他在后一封信中说：“Apud veteres iidem episcopi et presbyteri fuerunt, quia illud nomen dignitatis est, hoc aetatis.”（在古人中，主教与长老原是同一批人，因为前者是尊位的名称，后者是年龄的名称。）↩︎

  56. 金口约翰 (Chrysostom)，《腓立比书讲道》(Hom. i. in Ep. ad Philipp.) 第1篇（论《腓立比书》i. 1中的σὺν ἐπισκόποις*，这几个字意指同一会众中有若干主教，即长老）指出：τοὺς πρεσβυτέρους οὕτως ἐκάλεσε· τότε γὰρ τέως ἐκοινώνουν τοῖς ὀνόμασι.*。持同样见解的还有狄奥多勒 (Theodoret)，《腓立比书注释》(ad Phil.) i. 1及《提摩太书注释》(ad Tim.) iii. 1；伪安布罗斯 (Ambrosiaster)，《以弗所书注释》(ad Eph.) iv. 11；以及伪托奥古斯丁的《新旧约问题集》(Questiones V. et N.T.) 的作者，第101问。关于ἐπίσκοπος*与πρεσβύτερος*原初相同的整个问题，比较我的《使徒教会史》(History of the Apostolic Church)，§132（英译本，第522–531页），以及理查德·罗特 (Rich. Rothe)，《基督教会的起源》(Anfänge der christlichen Kirche)，第i卷，第207–217页。↩︎

  57. 米莱维的奥普塔图斯 (Optatus of Mileve) 的确称他们为教会中人 (ecclesiasticos viros)；但这并非指教牧人员 (clerici)，相反，他将两者加以区别，而是在广义上指与异教徒和异端有别的大公教会基督徒。关于这些所谓会众长老 (seniores plebis)，或平信徒长老，比较罗特博士 (Dr. Rothe)，《基督教会及其体制的起源》(Die Anfänge der christl. Kirche u. ihrer Verfassung)，第i卷，第227页及后的讨论。↩︎

  58. 比较《罗马书》xii. 1、12，以及我的《使徒教会史》(Hist. of the Apost. Church)，§135，第535页及后。↩︎

  59. 比较伯拉纠 (Pelagius)，《罗马书注释》(ad Rom.) xvi. 1。尼安德 (Neander)（第iii卷，第314页注；托里译本〔Torrey’s transl.〕，第ii卷，第158页）从第四次迦太基会议的一条教规推断，后一种习俗也盛行于西方，因为那里要求“viduae quae ad ministerium baptizandarum mulierum eliguntur”（被选来服侍将要受洗的妇女的寡妇），“ut possint apto et sano sermone docere imperitas et rusticas mulieres”（应当能够用适宜而纯正的话语，教导无知的乡野妇女）。↩︎

  60. 比较《狄奥多西法典》(Codex Theodos.)，第xvi卷，第ii题，第27法：“Nulla nisi emensis 60 annis secundum praeceptum apostoli ad diaconissarum consortium transferatur.”（依照使徒的命令，任何女子非满60岁，不得加入女执事的行列。）↩︎

  61. 《使徒宪章》(Const. Apost.)，第viii卷，第20章。我们已全文列出这篇祷文。尼安德 (Neander)（第iii卷，第322页注）省略了若干段落。这篇祷文以及所引迦克墩会议 (Chalcedon) 的教规，已使按立女执事的习俗成为无可争辩的事实。然而，尼西亚会议 (Nice) 第19条教规把女执事列在未受祝圣（χειροθεσία*）的平信徒之中，似乎与此抵触。因此，有人认为，女执事的按立是在尼西亚会议（Nicaenum，325年）之后才兴起的，尽管《使徒宪章》与此说相矛盾；另有人（如巴罗尼乌斯〔Baronius〕，以及近来的赫费勒〔Hefele〕，《教会会议史》〔Concilien-Gesch.〕，1855年，第i卷，第414页）则试图区分严格意义的χειροθεσία*与简单的祝福，以消除此矛盾。但女执事的祝圣，确实伴随着全体教牧人员在场时的按手；因为《使徒宪章》(Apost. Const.)，第viii卷，第19章，明确对主教说：Ἐπιθήσεις αὐτῇ τὰς χεῖρας, παρεστῶτος τοῦ πρεσβυτερίου καὶ τῶν διακόνων καὶ τῶν διακονισσῶν*。然而，矛盾就在那条尼西亚教规本身之中；因为（按照希腊文抄本）在紧前面的文字中，女执事被列在教牧人员之中，除非我们依从拉丁译文，在那里读作执事。尼安德则借着区分真正的女执事与被不当地如此称呼的寡妇，来解决这个困难。↩︎

  62. 这些书信见蒙福孔 (Montfaucon) 所编金口约翰著作本笃会版，第iii卷，第524—604页，以及洛姆勒 (Lomler) 所编《金口约翰重要著作选》(Joann. Chrysost. Opera praestantissima)，1840年，第168—252页。据蒙福孔所引（《著作集》〔Op.〕，第iii卷，第524页），佛提乌 (Photius) 认为，这十七封致奥林皮娅 (Olympias) 的信，在金口约翰的书信中是“longissimae, elegantissimae, omniumque utilissimae”（篇幅最长、文辞最优美，也最为有益的）。另比较蒙福孔关于奥林皮娅的序言。↩︎

  63. 所规定的只是祝福，以代替按立。441年第一次奥朗日会议 (Orange；Arausicana i.) 第26条教规规定：“Diaconae omnimodis non ordinandae [thus they had previously been ordained in Gaul also, and reckoned with the clergy]; si quae jam sunt, benedictioni, quae populo impenditur, capita submittant.”（女执事一概不得按立〔由此可见，此前在高卢，她们也曾受按立，并被列入教牧人员之中〕；若已有女执事，她们应当低头领受给予会众的祝福。）同样，517年在勃艮第 (Burgundy) 召开的埃帕翁会议 (Epaon) 第21条教规，以及533年第二次奥尔良会议 (Orleans) 第17、18条教规，也禁止按立女执事。↩︎

  74. 2 Ἱερὰ στολή*、ὡμοφόριον*、superhumerale、pallium（圣服、肩带），亦称ephod（以弗得；אֵפוֹד*、ἐπωμίς*）。以弗得（《出埃及记》xxviii. 6—11；xxxix. 2—5），连同附属于它的方形胸牌（חֹשֶׁן*，比较《出埃及记》xxviii. 15—30；xxxix. 8—21），是犹太大祭司的主要礼服，无疑是大主教肩带的先例，但比后者更为贵重。它由两片肩衣组成（与肩带及祭披相似），分别垂在上身的前后，以三色细麻精工织成，用金环和金链系住，又以金线和十二颗宝石华丽地装饰，宝石上刻着十二支派的名字。神圣的神谕之物乌陵和土明 (Urim and Thummim；七十士译本：δήλωσις καὶ ἀλήθεια*，《出埃及记》xxviii. 30)，是否就是胸牌上的十二颗宝石，学者们意见不一。比较维纳 (Winer)，《圣经实用词典》(Bibl. Reallex.)，以及W. 史密斯 (W. Smith)，《圣经词典》(Dictionary of the Bible)，“Urim and Thummim”条。↩︎

  75. 《演说》(Orat.) xlvii。狄奥多勒 (Theodoret)，《教会史》(Hist. eccl.) ii. 27开头也如此记述。据说马卡里乌斯 (Macarius) 在施洗时穿着这件饰金礼服。↩︎

  76. 阿米阿努斯·马尔凯利努斯 (Amm. Marcell.)，xxvii，第3章，367年条：“ut dotentur oblationibus matronarum procedantque vehiculis insidentes, circumspecte vestiti, epulas curantes profusas, adeo ut eorum convivia regales superent mensas.”（他们因贵妇的捐献而致富，出门乘车，衣着考究，讲究丰盛的宴席，以至其筵宴胜过王室的餐桌。）但随后，他以那些可敬的乡村主教的贫穷，与罗马高级教士的这种奢华作对照。↩︎

  77. 除阿米阿努斯 (Ammianus) 之外，哲罗姆也在其反对耶路撒冷的约翰 (John of Jerusalem) 的书中记述此事（《著作集》〔Opera〕，第ii卷，第415页，瓦拉尔西版〔ed. Vallars.〕）：“Miserabilis ille Praetextatus, qui designatus consul est mortuus, homo sacrilegus et idolorum cultor, solebat ludens beato papae Damaso dicere: ’Facite me Romanae urbis episcopum, et ero protinus Christianus.’ ”（那可悲的普赖特克斯塔图斯〔Praetextatus〕，在被任命为执政官后尚未就任便死去了；这个亵渎神圣、崇拜偶像的人，常戏谑地对蒙福的教宗达马苏斯〔Damasus〕说：“让我作罗马城的主教，我立刻就作基督徒。”）↩︎

  79. 各管区，或称副总督区，如下：

    I. 东方大行政区 (Praefectura Orientalis) 由五个管区组成：东方管区 (Oriens)，以安提阿为政治和教会首府；埃及管区 (Aegyptus)，以亚历山大为首府；亚细亚行省管区 (Asia proconsularis)，以以弗所为首府；本都管区 (Pontus)，以卡帕多西亚的凯撒利亚为首府；色雷斯管区 (Thracia)，以赫拉克利亚 (Heraklea)、后来以君士坦丁堡为首府。

    1. 伊利里亚大行政区 (Praefectura Illyrica)，以帖撒罗尼迦为首府，只有马其顿 (Macedonia) 和达契亚 (Dacia) 两个管区。

    2. 意大利大行政区 (Praefectura Italica) 包括：罗马管区 (Roma)，即意大利南部和地中海诸岛，或所谓近郊诸行省；意大利管区 (Italia)，或称意大利副总督区，以梅迪奥拉努姆 (Mediolanum，即米兰〔Milan〕) 为中心；西伊利里亚管区 (Illyricum occidentale)，以西尔米乌姆 (Sirmium) 为首府；西非洲管区 (Africa occidentalis)，以迦太基为首府。

    3. 高卢大行政区 (Praefectura Gallica) 包括：高卢管区 (Gallia)，以特雷维里 (Treveri，即特里尔〔Trier〕) 和卢格杜努姆 (Lugdunum，即里昂〔Lyons〕) 为首府；西班牙管区 (Hispania)，以希斯帕利斯 (Hispalis，即塞维利亚〔Sevilla〕) 为首府；不列颠管区 (Britannnia)，以埃博拉库姆 (Eboracum，即约克〔York〕) 为首府。

    ↩︎

  80. 例如，东方管区有五个行省，埃及有九个，本都有十三个，高卢有十七个，西班牙有七个。比较维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，《教会地理与统计》(Kirchl. Geogr. u. Statistik)，第i卷，第67页及后；该处列出了所有行省，按我们此处的目的，无须一一列举。↩︎

Ancyr.) 第13条教规（禁止他们按立长老和执事）；公元341年安提阿会议 (Concil. Antioch.) 第10条教规（同样的禁令）；320至372年之间的老底嘉会议 (Conc. Laodic.) 第57条教规（禁止新设乡村主教区）；以及公元343年撒狄迦会议 (Conc. Sardic.) 第6条教规（将他们完全废除）。↩︎

  82. 第6条教规：……ἵνα μὴ κατευτελίζηται τὸ τοῦ ἐπισκόπου ὄνομα καὶ ἡ αὐθεντία*；或按拉丁译文：“Ne vilescat nomen episcopi et auctoritas.”（免得主教的名号和权柄受到贬损。）比较赫费勒 (Hefele)，第i卷，第556页。这条教规前半部分的希腊文本与拉丁文本虽有差异，却不影响其中禁止任命乡村主教的规定。↩︎

  86. 居普良 (Cyprian) 在《书信》(Epist.) 45中论到自己所属的迦太基教省说：“Latius fusa est nostra provincia; habet enim Numidiam et Mauretaniam sibi cohaerentes.”（我们的教省幅员辽阔，因为努米底亚〔Numidia〕和毛里塔尼亚〔Mauretania〕也与之相连。）↩︎

  87. Πατριάρχης*；patriarcha（牧首）；有时也沿用政治术语，称为ἔξαρχος*。“牧首”一名，原用于以色列人的先祖（《希伯来书》vii. 4指亚伯拉罕；《使徒行传》vii. 8及下节指雅各的十二个儿子；ii. 29指大卫，因为他是大卫家弥赛亚王朝的创立者），在东方教会起初是一般主教的尊称（如拿先斯的格列高利〔Gregory Nazianzen〕和尼撒的格列高利〔Gregory of Nyssa〕所用），但在君士坦丁堡会议（381年）之后，尤其在迦克墩会议（451年）之后，逐渐用作正式职衔，并限于五位最显要的都主教。在西方，若干都主教，尤其是阿奎莱亚 (Aquileia) 的主教，也以此作为荣誉称号（honoris causa）。罗马主教不愿接受这一特定称谓，因为它使自己与其他牧首处于同等地位，而更喜欢papa（教宗）一名。“牧首”一称表明的是寡头式教会治理；“教宗”则表明君主式治理。↩︎

  89. 比较维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第206页及后。齐格勒 (Ziegler) 声称，553年第五次大公会议将腓尼基的贝里图斯 (Berytus) 和叙利亚的鲁巴 (Ruba) 两个都主教区划入耶路撒冷牧首的辖区；维尔奇引述此说，似乎也表示赞同，但此说看来有误。会议记录中全然不见鲁巴，而贝里图斯属于第一腓尼基行省 (Phoenicia prima)，因此隶属安提阿牧首区。勒基安 (Le Quien) 对耶路撒冷 (Hierosolyma) 牧首区曾有过这样的扩张一无所知。↩︎

  94. 比较维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第232页及下页，以及第ii卷，第469页。↩︎

  95. 《非洲教会教规汇编》(Cod. can. eccl. Afr.)，第39条教规，转引自尼安德 (Neander)，第iii卷，第335页（德文版）。↩︎

  96. 因此，教宗尼古拉 (Nicolas) 在866年致保加利亚王公博戈里斯 (Bogoris) 的信中，只愿承认罗马、亚历山大和安提阿的主教为严格意义上的牧首，因为他们主持的是使徒所建的教会；而君士坦丁堡并非使徒所创立，甚至在所有会议中最受尊崇的尼西亚会议也未提及它；耶路撒冷固然为这些会议所提及，却只是以埃利亚 (Aelia) 之名出现。↩︎

  97. 在最古老的拉丁文《教规汇编》(Cod. canonum；载于曼西〔Mansi〕，第vi卷，第1186页) 中，这条教规之前有一句重要的话：Ecclesia Romana semper habuit primatum（罗马教会一向拥有首位权）。然而，这句话显然是伪加的，原本并非教规本身的一部分，而是一项题记，表达了罗马方面从尼西亚教规中所作的推论。比较吉泽勒 (Gieseler)，i. 2，§93，注1；以及赫费勒 (Hefele)，《教会会议史》(Hist. of Councils)，第i卷，第384页及后。↩︎

  98. 格林伍德 (Greenwood) 也如此看待此事，见《彼得的座位》(Cathedra Petri)，1859年，第i卷，第181页：“这条教规的目的，显然不是赋予亚历山大教会任何新的管辖权，而只是确认它惯有的特权。作为说明，它把这项特权，无论其具体内容为何，与罗马和安提阿另外两个管区教会的特权置于同等地位。此外，教规的措辞表明，这种权利要求的根据，除惯例之外别无其他；而各管区的惯例，都限于该管区本身的地域界限之内。即使在这些界限以内，各种惯有的权利和特权可能有所不同，但在这些界限以外，任何性质的管辖权，都绝不可能凭借这条教规而存在。”↩︎

  99. 第一次君士坦丁堡会议 (Conc. Constant. i.) 第3条教规：Τὸν μέντοι Κωνσταντινουπόλεως ἐπίσκοπον ἔχειν τὰ πρεσβεῖα τῆς τιμῆς, μετὰ τὸν τῆς Ῥώμης ἐπίσκοπον, διὰ τὸ εἶναι αὐτὴν νέαν Ῥώμην*。（然而，君士坦丁堡的主教应当享有仅次于罗马主教的尊荣地位，因为该城是新罗马。）苏格拉底 (Socrates)，v. 8，以及索佐门 (Sozomen)，vii. 9，也引述了这条教规；迦克墩会议又确认了它（见下文）。所以，巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius)（《编年史》〔Annal.〕，381年条，第35、36项）质疑其真实性，必定纯粹出于教义上的偏见。↩︎

  100. 诚然，上述教规并未明说后一点，似乎只谈及荣誉上的优先地位。但君士坦丁堡的主教们，以及历史学家苏格拉底 (Socrates)（v. 8）和狄奥多勒 (Theodoret)（《书信》〔Epist.〕86，致弗拉维安〔ad Flavianum〕），都如此理解这条教规；迦克墩会议也作了这样的解释（第28条教规）。不过，君士坦丁堡主教与赫拉克利亚 (Heraclea) 都主教之间的关系，长期未定；在403年处理金口约翰事件的橡树会议 (ad Quercum) 上，虽然亚历山大牧首狄奥菲卢斯 (Theophilus) 在场，主持会议的却是赫拉克利亚的保罗 (Paul)。比较勒基安 (Le Quien)，第i卷，第18页；以及维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第139页。↩︎

  101. 《教会史》(H. E.)，第v卷，第28章。↩︎

  102. 据索佐门 (Sozomen) 说，他任立了十三位主教；据亚历山大的狄奥菲卢斯 (Theophilus) 在橡树会议 (ad Quercum) 上的说法，则是十七位；而这一行为被指控为前所未闻的罪行。参见维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第141页。↩︎

  103. 苏格拉底 (Socrates)，《教会史》(H. E.)，第vii卷，第28章，其中附带提及这样一项法律。然而，赫勒斯滂 (Hellespont) 的基齐库斯 (Cyzicus) 居民却违犯了这项法律，因为他们认为这只是阿提库斯 (Atticus) 个人的特权。↩︎

  104. 在君士坦丁堡 (Constantinople) 主教行使教会管辖权的蛮族部落中，有博斯普鲁斯 (Bosphorus) 一带的匈人 (Huns)，其王戈尔达 (Gorda) 在查士丁尼 (Justinian) 时代受洗；有于527年接受基督教信仰的赫鲁利人 (Herulians)；还有黑海 (Euxine sea) 沿岸的阿巴斯吉人 (Abasgians) 和阿兰人 (Alanians)，他们约在同一时期接受了从君士坦丁堡派来的祭司。比较维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第144及145页。↩︎

  105. 罗马使节受到的这一纠正，极不合罗马天主教历史学家、尤其是教皇至上派历史学家的心意，以致巴莱里尼兄弟 (Ballerini) 在其所编《大利奥著作集》第iii卷，第xxxvii页及后，甚至赫费勒 (Hefele) 在《教会议史》(Conciliengesch.) 第i卷，第385页及第ii卷，第522页，都毫无证据地断言，迦克墩会议 (Chalcedon) 希腊文议事录中的有关段落，是后人插入的。赫费勒不得不承认，拉丁译本偏离了尼西亚 (Nice) 第六条教规的原文；不过，他认为，在迦克墩并未宣读希腊文本，因为就连这一文本也反对提高君士坦丁堡的地位，因而有利于罗马使节。但罗马使节，以及在抗议迦克墩第二十八条决议时的利奥 (Leo)，都把重点放在罗马方面增添的文字——Ecclesia Romana semper habuit primatum（罗马教会向来拥有首席权）——之上，并认为，任何其他牧首与罗马主教地位平等，都与此不相容。既然公认使节曾援引尼西亚教规，希腊人就必须先回应这一援引，然后才能转而诉诸君士坦丁堡会议的教规。只有两者合在一起，才构成对罗马方面抗议的充分答复。↩︎

  106. 曼西 (Mansi)，第vii卷，第446–454页；阿尔杜安 (Harduin)，第ii卷，第639–643页；赫费勒 (Hefele)，第ii卷，第524、525页。↩︎

  107. 利奥 (Leo)，《书信》(Epist.) 第104、105及106封（另一编号为 Ep. 第78–80封）。比较赫费勒 (Hefele)，前引书，第ii卷，第530页及后。↩︎

  108. 《启示录》三11。↩︎

  109. 阿尔杜安 (Harduin)，第vii卷，第23页；施勒克 (Schröckh)，第xvii卷，第43页；以及赫费勒 (Hefele)，第ii卷，第544页。↩︎

  110. 比较优西比乌 (Eusebius)，《教会史》(H. E.)，第v卷，第23章；他本人就是凯撒利亚 (Caesarea) 的都主教。他列举了耶路撒冷主教的承继次序，也列举了罗马、亚历山大和安提阿的，却略去了凯撒利亚的。↩︎

  111. Ἀκολουθία τῆς τιμῆς*；对此有不同的解释。比较赫费勒 (Hefele)，第i卷，第389页及下页。↩︎

  112. Τὰ πρεσβεῖα τῆς τιμῆς*……διὰ τὸ εἶναι αὐτὴν*〔即君士坦丁堡〕νέαν Ῥώμην*。比较§ 56。↩︎

  113. οἰκομενικὸς πατριάρχης*，即 universalis episcopus（普世主教）这一称号，此前东方牧首已用作谄媚之辞；后来的罗马主教也毫无顾忌地采用它，不理会贵格利一世 (Gregory I.) 的抗议。教皇贵格利一世和利奥九世 (Leo IX.) 声称，迦克墩会议曾授予罗马主教利奥 universal episcopus（普世主教）的称号，而他拒绝接受；这一说法是错误的。无论在会议的议事录中，还是在利奥的书信中，都找不到任何这样的痕迹。在议事录中，利奥被称为ὁ ἁγιώτατος καὶ μακαριώτατος ἀρχιεπίσκοπος τῆς μεγάλης καὶ πρεσβυτέρας Ῥώμης*（伟大而古老的罗马至圣至福的大主教）；但在利奥送交高卢主教的拉丁文议事录中，这一称号被扩充为：“Sanctus et beatissimus Papa, caput universalis ecclesiae, Leo”（至圣至福的教皇、普世教会之首利奥）。迦克墩会议的教皇使节署名自称：Vicarii apostolici universalis ecclesiae papae（普世教会教皇的使徒代理人），希腊人将其译为：τῆς οἰκουμενικῆς* ἐκκλησία*" ἐπισκόπου*。贵格利一世的错误很可能由此而来。教皇们希望成为 papae universalis ecclesiae（普世教会的教皇），而不是 episcopi 或 patriarchae universales（普世主教或普世牧首）；这无疑是因为，后两种称谓使他们与东方牧首处于同等地位。比较吉泽勒 (Gieseler)，第i卷第2分册，第192页注20及第228页注72；以及赫费勒 (Hefele)，第ii卷，第525页及下页。↩︎

  114. 《书信》(Epist.) 第113封，致教皇利奥一世 (Leo I.)。↩︎

  115. 使徒安得烈将福音传到古拜占庭 (Byzantium) 的说法，是后世全不可靠的传说。↩︎

  116. 一个例外，是亚流派 (Arian) 腓力斯二世 (Felix II) 短暂的教皇任期；355年，皇帝君士坦提乌斯 (Constantius) 趁利贝里乌斯 (Liberius) 被放逐之时，强行将他扶上教皇之位，有些人认为他是非法的对立教皇。不过，关于他的记载彼此矛盾极大，甚至罗马天主教历史学家的意见也是如此。利贝里乌斯也曾在357年短暂地陷入亚流主义，以求获准从流放中归来。另一个较晚的例外是教皇霍诺里乌斯 (Honorius)，就连681年在君士坦丁堡召开的第六次大公会议，也因他持基督一志论 (Monothelite) 异端而将他咒诅。↩︎

  117. 试回想居普良 (Cyprian) 著作中插入的教皇主义段落；罗马方面对尼西亚第六条教规的扩充；借尼西亚会议之名及其权威援引撒狄迦 (Sardica) 教规；以及后来臭名昭著的伪伊西多尔教令集 (pseudo-Isidorian decretals)。诚然，教皇们不是这些伪造文本的最初作者，但他们为达到自己的目的，毫无顾忌地反复利用它们。↩︎

  118. 325年尼西亚会议 (Concil. Nicaean.) 第6条教规，鲁菲努斯 (Rufinus) 的拉丁译文（《教会史》〔Hist. Eccl.〕第x卷，第6章）：“Et ut apud Alexandria et in urbe Roma vetusta consuetudo servetur, ut vel ille Ægypti, vel hic suburbicariarum ecclesiarum sollicitudinem gerat.”（亚历山大及罗马城应遵守古老惯例，前者负责照管埃及，后者负责照管城郊各教会。）suburb. eccl. 这些字在希腊原文中并不存在，是四世纪末用拉丁文对罗马牧首辖区所作的界定。自十七世纪以来，这些字在学者之间引起了长期的争论。法学家戈托弗雷杜斯 (Gothofredus) 及其友人萨尔马修斯 (Salmasius) 将 regiones suburbicariae（城郊地区）限于 Praefectus Urbis（罗马城长官）的小辖区，即罗马城及其周围直到第一百里程碑的地区；耶稣会士西尔蒙 (Sirmond) 则将其扩展到 Vicarius Urbis（罗马城代理长官）大得多的行政辖区，即以下十个行省：坎帕尼亚 (Campania)、图斯基亚及翁布里亚 (Tuscia with Umbria)、城郊皮凯努姆 (Picenum suburbicarium)、瓦莱里亚 (Valeria)、萨姆尼乌姆 (Samnium)、阿普利亚及卡拉布里亚 (Apulia with Calabria)、卢卡尼亚及布鲁蒂 (Lucania and Brutii)、西西里 (Sicilia)、撒丁 (Sardinia) 和科西嘉 (Corsica)。尼西亚会议第六条教规将罗马主教与亚历山大主教相比，这一点有利于后一种看法；因为亚历山大教区同样也横跨数个行省。然而，Prisca（一部五世纪中叶的拉丁文教规汇编）在翻译这条教规时，或许说中了实情：“Antiqui moris est ut urbis Romae episcopus habeat principatum, ut suburbicaria loca〔此处无疑指 Praefectus 的较小辖区〕et omnem provinciam suam〔即 Vicarius 的较大辖区，或范围更广而不明确的地区〕sollicitudine sua gubernet.”（按照古老惯例，罗马城主教享有首席权，负责治理城郊地区及其全部辖区。）比较曼西 (Mansi)，《会议汇编》(Coll. Conc.)，第vi卷，第1127页；以及赫费勒 (Hefele)，第i卷，第380页及后。↩︎

  119. 按照帝国的政治区划，五世纪的罗马牧首区包括三个大行政区，下分八个行政管区和六十九个行省。它们是：（1）意大利大行政区，包括意大利、伊利里库姆 (Illyricum) 和非洲三个管区；（2）高卢大行政区 (praefectum Galliarum)，包括高卢、西班牙和不列颠三个管区；（3）伊利里库姆大行政区（不可与隶属意大利大行政区的伊利里亚〔Illyria〕行省混淆），879年以后，它虽以东伊利里库姆 (Illyricum orientale) 之名脱离了西罗马帝国，却仍在教会事务上与罗马保持联系，包括马其顿 (Macedonia) 和达契亚 (Dacia) 两个管区。比较维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，前引书，第i卷，第67页及后；马森 (Maassen)，第125页；以及赫费勒 (Hefele)，第i卷，第383页。↩︎

  133. 贵格利 (Greg.)，《书信》(Ep.)，第i卷，第41封；曼西 (Mansi)，第ix卷，第1059页。比较维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第71页。↩︎

  134. 这种分歧分别体现在《大利奥著作集》的两个版本中：一是法国人帕斯基耶·凯内尔 (Pasquier Quesnel) 的版本；他是高卢主义者和詹森派信徒（1681年被放逐，1719年卒于布鲁塞尔），其版本还收入了阿尔勒的希拉里 (Hilary of Arles) 的著作及一篇为他辩护的文章（巴黎〔Par.〕，1675年，2卷），1676年禁书目录部甚至未经阅读便予以谴责；另一版本由巴莱里尼 (Ballerini) 兄弟二人为反对前一版本而出版（威尼斯〔Ven.〕，1755–1757年，3卷），代表意大利教皇至上派的立场。关于阿尔勒的希拉里 (Hilarius Arelatensis，不可与普瓦捷的希拉里〔Hilarius Pictaviensis〕、纳博讷的希拉里〔Hilarius Narbonensis〕及其他同名者混淆) 与教皇利奥的这场争论，还可比较马赛的霍诺拉图斯 (Honoratus Massiliensis) 约于490年所写的《希拉里传》(Vita Hilarii)（刊于曼西，第vi卷，第461页及后，以及《圣徒行传》〔Acta Sanct.〕5月5日条）；佩特尔 (Perthel) 载于伊尔根 (Illgen) 所编1843年《历史神学杂志》(Zeitschrift for Hist. Theol.) 的文章；格林伍德 (Greenwood)，前引书，第i卷，第350–356页；米尔曼 (Milman)，《拉丁基督教》(Lat. Christianity)，第i卷，第269–276页（美国版）；以及韦策尔 (Wetzer) 与韦尔特 (Welte)《教会辞典》(Kirchenlexic) 第v卷，第181页及后的“希拉里”条。↩︎

  135. “Nisi magnitudo causae etiam nostrum exquirat examen.”（除非案件的重大性要求也由我们加以审查。）吉泽勒 (Gieseler)，第i卷第2分册，第218页；格林伍德 (Greenwood)，第i卷，第299页。↩︎

  136. 比较公元422年博尼法斯一世 (Boniface I.) 致纳博讷的希拉里（并非阿尔勒的希拉里）的《书信》第12封 (Bonifacii I Epist. 12 ad Hilarium Narbon.)，载于吉泽勒 (Gieseler)，第219页。博尼法斯在这里赞同尼西亚的原则，即每位都主教应只治理一个行省。格林伍德 (Greenwood) 忽略了这一变化，因而根据佐西穆斯 (Zosimus) 的任命，认为希拉里完全有理。但即使这一任命仍然有效，罢免一位主教仍属 causa major（重大案件），希拉里作为教皇的代理人，本应将此事呈交教皇批准。↩︎

  137. 利奥 (Leo)，《书信》(Epist.) 第10封（另一编号为第89封），致维埃纳行省诸主教 (ad Episc. provinciae Viennensis)。这对真正基督徒的立场是何等可怕的歪曲！↩︎

  138. 教皇维吉利乌斯 (Vigil，539–555年)、伯拉纠 (Pelagius，555–559年) 和大贵格利 (Gregory the Great)，除了授予阿尔勒 (Arles) 大主教披带之外，也授予他教皇代理职权 (vices)。比较维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第71页及下页。↩︎

  139. 无论如何，无法确证双方曾经和解。希拉里 (Hilary) 的门徒兼传记作者，在他死后约四十年为他罩上圣者的光环；据此人的记述，希拉里的确曾采取若干和解举措，派遣两位祭司为代表，携信前往罗马长官奥克西利亚里斯 (Auxiliaris) 那里。后者努力居间调停，却也让代表们明白，希拉里以其激烈无畏的言辞，太深地刺伤了罗马人娇嫩的耳朵。在利奥的信中，希拉里又受到一项新的指控，说他在凯利多尼乌斯 (Celidonius) 被罢免以后，侵犯了主教普罗耶克图斯 (Projectus) 的权利。希拉里在这场争论后不久便去世了（449年）。沃特兰 (Waterland) 曾将《亚他那修信经》归于他名下，却没有充分根据。↩︎

  140. 比较吉泽勒 (Gieseler)，第i卷第2分册，第215页及后；以及维尔奇 (Wiltsch)，第i卷，第72页及后、第431页及后。↩︎

  141. papa 这一名称——有人认为是 pater patrum（众父之父）的缩写，但更可能像同源的 abbas、πάππας* 或 πάπας*、pa-pa 一样，只是模仿婴儿最初的牙牙学语，因此相当于“父亲”——在西方很长时期内，是每一位主教作为属灵之父的尊称；但在第五世纪以后，它成了牧首特有的尊号，再后来则专属于罗马主教，而且赋予他特别崇高的意义，视之为全教会之父。比较杜康热 (Du Cange)，《词汇集》(Glossar.)，Papa 和 Pater Patrum 条；以及霍夫曼 (Hoffmann)，《通用辞典》(Lexic. univers.)，第iv卷，第561页。意大利语和西班牙语的 papa、法语的 pape、英语的 pope，以及德语的 Papst 或 Pabst，也都用于这一专属的意义。相反，在希腊和俄罗斯教会中，所有祭司都称为 Popes（源于 πάπας*、papa）。apostolicus（使徒的）、vicarius Christi（基督的代理）、summus pontifex（最高祭司）、sedes apostolica（使徒座）这些称号，在相当长时期内曾授予不同的主教及其主教座，但后来却被罗马主教宣称为自己所专有。↩︎

  142. 《马太福音》十六18：Σὺ εἶ Πέτρος, καὶ ἐπὶ ταύτῃ τῇ πέτρᾳ*［注意这里从阳性变为阴性，从人转到事物，或转到所承认的真理；这一变化在英语和德语译本中消失了］οἰκοδομήσω μου τὴν ἐκκλησίαν, καὶ πύλαι ᾅδου οὐ κατισχύσουσιν αὐτῆς*。比较各家注释，尤其迈耶 (Meyer)、兰格 (Lange)、奥尔福德 (Alford)、华兹华斯 (Wordsworth) 对此处的注释（ad loc.），以及我的《使徒教会史》(Hist. of the Apost. Church)，§ 90、94（纽约版，第350页及后、第374页及后）。↩︎

  143. 比较第i卷，§ 110。↩︎

  144. 巴罗尼乌斯 (Baronius)，《编年史》(Annal.)，1080年条，第xi卷，第704页。↩︎

  145. 哲罗姆 (Hieronymus)，《驳约维尼安》(Adv. Jovin.)，第ii卷，第38章（《著作集》〔Opera〕，第ii卷，第382页），他在那里向罗马说：“Ad te loquar, quae scriptam in fronte blasphemiam Christi confessione delesti.”（我要对你说话，你因承认基督，抹去了写在额上的亵渎之名。）普罗斯珀 (Prosper)：“Eterna cum dicitur quae temporalis est, utique nomen est blasphemiae.”（将有暂时之限的称为永恒，无疑是亵渎的称号。）比较皮珀 (Piper)，前引书，第46页。↩︎

  146. 金口约翰 (Chrysostom) 对《帖撒罗尼迦后书》二7的解释亦如此；哲罗姆 (Hieronymus)，《书信》(Ep.) 第cxxi封，第11问（第i卷，第880页及下页）；奥古斯丁 (Augustine)，《上帝之城》(De Civit. Dei)，第xx卷，第19章。↩︎

  147. 《论多纳徒派的分裂》(De schismate Donatistarum)，第ii卷，第2、3章，以及第vii卷，第3章。此书写于西里基乌斯 (Siricius) 任罗马主教期间，因此约在384年。↩︎

  148. 安布罗斯 (Ambr.)，《彼得与保罗节讲道》第二篇（Sermo ii. in festo Petri et Pauli）：“In urbe Romae, quae principatum et caput obtinet nationum: scilicet ut ubi caput superstitionis erat, illic caput quiesceret sanctitatis, et ubi gentilium principes habitabant, illic ecclesiarum principes morerentur.”（在罗马城，就是那在万国中居首为头的城：使从前为迷信之首的地方，如今成为圣洁之首安息的地方；外邦人首领从前居住的地方，如今成为众教会首领殉世的地方。）《诗篇》第40篇注（In Ps. 40）：“Ipse est Petrus cui dixit: Tu es Petrus ... ubi ergo Patrus, ibi ecclesia; ubi ecclesia, ibi mulla mors, sed vita eterna.”（他就是那位彼得，主曾对他说：你是彼得……所以，彼得在哪里，教会就在哪里；教会在哪里，那里就没有死亡，只有永生。）比较下文引自奥古斯丁的文字中，他那首《晨祷颂》的诗句。但在另一处，他也将磐石解释为基督，见《路加福音》九20注：“Petra est Christus”（磐石就是基督），等等。↩︎

  149. 《论主的道成肉身》(De incarnat. Domini)，第4章：“Primatum confessionis utique, non honoris, primatum fidei, non ordinis.”（当然是认信上的首位，而非尊荣上的首位；是信仰上的首位，而非品级上的首位。）↩︎

  150. 《论圣灵》(De Spiritu S.)，第ii卷，第12章：“Nec Paulus inferior Petro, quamvis ille ecclesiae fundamentum.”（保罗也不低于彼得，尽管后者是教会的根基。）《彼得与保罗节讲道》第二篇（Sermo ii. in festo P. et P.），紧接上引段落之前说：“Ergo beati Petrus et Paulus eminent inter universos apostolos, et peculiari quadam praerogativa praecellunt. Verum inter ipsos, quis cui praeponatur, incertum est. Puto enim illos aequales esse meritis, qui aequales sunt passione.”（所以，有福的彼得和保罗在众使徒中卓然出众，享有某种特殊的优越地位。但他们两人究竟谁应居先，却不能确定。因为我认为，他们既同样受难，功绩也应相等。）奥古斯丁也曾称保罗，而非彼得，为 caput et princeps apostolorum（使徒之首和领袖），又在另一处说他 tanti apostolatus meruit principatum（配得如此伟大的使徒职分之首位）。↩︎

  151. 哲罗姆 (Hieron.)，《阿摩司书注》(in Amos)，六12：“Petra Christus est qui donavit apostolis suis, ut ipsi quoque petrae vocentur.”（磐石就是基督，他赐给自己的使徒，使他们也得称为磐石。）又在另一处说：“Ecclesia Catholica super Petram Christum stabili radici fundata est.”（大公教会牢牢扎根，建立在磐石基督之上。）↩︎

  152. 《驳约维尼安》(Adv. Jovin.)，第i卷，第26章（瓦拉尔西〔Vallars.〕版，第ii卷，第279页），答复约维尼安 (Jovinian) 援引彼得以支持婚姻的论证：“At dicis: super Petrum fundatur ecclesia; licet id ipsum in alio loco super omnes apostolos fiat, et cuncti claves regni coelorum accipiant, et ex aequo super eos fortitudo ecclesiae solidetur, tamen propterea inter duodecim unus eligitur, ut capite constituto, schismatis tollatur occasio.”（但你说：教会建立在彼得之上；虽然别处也同样说建立在全体使徒之上，而且他们全都领受天国的钥匙，教会的力量也同样坚立在他们之上，然而从十二人中选出一人，正是为了设立首领，消除分裂的机会。）《致教皇达马苏书》（Epist. xv. ad Damasum papam，瓦拉尔西版，第i卷，第37页）亦如此。↩︎

  153. 比较《致埃万格鲁斯书》（Epist. 146，瓦拉尔西版，第i卷，第1076页；或本笃会版〔Bened.〕第101封，另一编号为85；ad Evangelum）：“Ubicunque fuerit episcopus, sive Romae, sive Eugubii, sive Constantinopoli, sive Rhegii, sive Alexandriae, sive Tanis［有意将最强大和最不起眼的主教区并列］, ejusdem est meriti, ejusdem est et sacerdotii. Potentia divitiarum et paupertatis humilitas vel sublimiorem vel inferiorem episcopum non facit. Caeterum omnes apostolorum successores sunt.”（主教无论在哪里，无论在罗马、尤古比乌姆、君士坦丁堡、雷吉乌姆、亚历山大，还是塔尼斯，其地位相同，祭司职分也相同。财富带来的权势与贫穷造成的卑微，并不能使一位主教更高或更低。何况他们全都是使徒的继承人。）↩︎

  154. 比较上文 § 52。J. 克雷吉·罗伯逊 (J. Craigie Robertson)，《截至590年的基督教会史》(Hist. of the Christian Church to 590，伦敦，1854年)，第286页注，在圣哲罗姆《书信》第125封中，发现了一项反对教皇权主张的显著反面证据：“信中以走兽、蜜蜂和鹤的本能习性，以以扫和雅各、罗慕路斯和雷穆斯的争斗，以及一国只有一个皇帝、一省只有一个法官、一家只有一个主人、一船只有一个舵手、一军只有一个统帅、一教会只有一个主教、一个总长老和一个总执事，来要求修士服从一位首领；但却没有提到那位普世唯一的主教。”↩︎

  155. 《致教皇达马苏书》第xv封（另一编号为57；Ep. xv. ad Damasum papam；瓦拉尔西版，第i卷，第37页及下页）：“Facessat invidia: Romani culminis recedat ambitio, cum successore piscatoris et discipulo crucis loquor. Ego nullum primum, nisi Christum sequens, Beatitudini tuae, id est cathedrae Petri, communione consocior. Super illam petram aedificatam ecclesiam scio. Quicunque extra hanc domum agnum comederit, profanus est. Si quis in Noe arca non fuerit, peribit regnante diluvio.”（让嫉妒退去，让对罗马至高地位的野心离开；我是在与那渔夫的继承人、十字架的门徒说话。我不以任何人为首，只跟从基督；我与你这位至福者，也就是与彼得的座位，联结于团契之中。我知道教会建立在那磐石之上。凡在这家以外吃羔羊的，便是不圣洁的。凡不在挪亚方舟里的，当洪水横行时，必然灭亡。）↩︎

  156. 哲罗姆 (Hier.)，《加拉太书注》(Com. in Ep. ad Galat.)，二11、12（瓦拉尔西版，第vii卷，第409栏）：“Non quod aliud significat Petrus, aliud Cephas, sed quo quam nos Latine et Graece petram vocemus, hanc Hebraei et Syri, propter linguae inter se viciniam, Cephan, nuncupent.”（并不是 Petrus 和 Cephas 各有不同含义，而是我们在拉丁语和希腊语中称为 petram 的磐石，希伯来人和叙利亚人因彼此语言相近，称之为 Cephan。）↩︎

  159. 《约翰福音讲论》(Tract. in Evang. Joannis)，第124篇，§ 5。吉泽勒博士 (Dr. Gieseler) 等人曾引录原文，见其著作第i卷第2分册，第210页（第4版），但有几处无关紧要的省略。↩︎
